# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-04-06_TotalExtraction_23781623.pdf
   
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
April 26, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, 
Richard Johnson, Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White.  
Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support 
received by Mr. Johnson and Mr. White, agenda item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  
Upon request by Mr. Johnson, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Segrave and Mr. White, 
agenda item #23(b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve 
remaining items, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on April 12, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for the City of Cheyenne’s Annual Action Plan 
Fiscal Year 2021, prepared by the Cheyenne Housing and Community Development Office.  
Deanne Widauf, Housing and Community Development, provided a brief overview of the history 
and purpose of the Community Development Block Grant program, noting the proposed plan is 
consistent with the 5-year Comprehensive Consolidated Plan completed in 2020, and stated the 
anticipated allocation funding amount is $478,351.00 and $127,319.00 in recaptured funding.  Ms. 
Widauf reviewed information on the grant application process; public notifications and 
opportunities for input; role of the Advisory Council in reviewing and recommending projects to 
be funded and explained the projects that are recommended for funding.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, land 
located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney Road.  
Reporting for Public Services Committee, Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from County A-2 Agricultural to AG Agricultural for 
land located northwest of and adjacent to the intersection of Dell Range Boulevard and Whitney 

   
Road.  Dr. Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.   
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Municipal Code 
of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Rescinding Footnote 1 in Ordinance No. 4314 
appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming for the various purposes in conducting 
the municipal government of said city and fixing the amount of General and Special Taxes as part 
of the revenue required to meet the said appropriation, all for the fiscal year beginning July 1, 
2020, and ending June 30, 2021.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by 
Mr. Segrave.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Michael O’Donnell explained rescinding footnote 1 
eliminates the cap on employees and noted there is no substitute document. Mayor Collins 
explained upon inquiry that there will be a large upcoming development project, and there is a 
need to hire a plan reviewer and two inspectors to handle the project.  Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, provided information on the revenue amount the city will receive in relation to the 
project.  Mayor Collins confirmed the footnote would expire at the end of the current fiscal year 
and that if the ordinance is approved, the footnote will be removed for the remaining months of 
this fiscal year.  Mayor Collins further expressed the need to recruit and hire necessary employees 
prior to June 30, 2021.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule – referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80% -- 
referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming – 
referred to Finance Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Roybal) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing submission of an application to the Wyoming Department 
of Transportation for a Wyoming Rural Public Transportation Program Grant to provide the local 
match for operating funds for the City of Cheyenne for its Transit Program, as authorized by 49 
U.S.C.§5311.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#6138) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Adopting the City of Cheyenne’s Statement of Design Standards, 
Naming, Memorial and Gifting Policy.  Dr. Rinne moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Cook.  
Comment was made on the standards, page 2, under “Naming or Renaming for Exceptional 
Individuals”, 3rd and 4th lines pertaining to naming a facility after a person is deceased and it was 
noted the city hasn’t followed that practice in the past. Dr. Rinne moved to amend the standards 

   
by deleting the sentences relating to naming rights after a person is deceased, seconded by Mr. 
Johnson. Teresa Moore, Director of Community Recreation and Events, upon inquiry, advised the 
naming standard used by their department used to require that 7 years must pass after a person had 
died prior to facility naming consideration and that at the request of Mr. Cook and Mr. Esquibel, 
it was reduced to 2 years.  It was noted this document was crafted 10 years ago by the Parks & 
Recreation Department and was a common component based on past situations where a facility 
had to be renamed and was designed to address certain situations that can be handled differently.  
Comment was made that other agencies in the community limit naming rights to people who are 
deceased.  Mr. O’Donnell confirmed upon inquiry that this proposed policy serves as a guide but 
allows for flexibility.  Comments supporting the amendment were voiced.  Motion to amend 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Roybal and 
Mr. Segrave voting “no”.  It was advised that a request was made to the Historical Preservation 
Board to research and provide history on naming rights, noting the rich history the Cheyenne 
community has.  Comment was made that city owned facilities should not have a person’s name 
affiliated with it and that government entities have different policies relating to naming rights.  
Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the 
exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  (#6139) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Setting the City’s priorities for their allocation on the 1% Specific 
Purpose Sales and Use Tax in Laramie County, State of Wyoming, that would be put together by 
the County Attorney for a Special Election to be held on November 2, 2021.  Amber Ash, 
Downtown Development Authority (DDA) Director, expressed appreciation to the governing body 
for their consideration for downtown district improvements noting many downtown infrastructure 
elements have deteriorated.  Reporting for Committee of the Whole, Mr. White moved to adopt, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White moved to amend by substitute dated April 22, 2021 to 
include the following amendments made at the April 21st Committee of the Whole meeting:  
Revised entitlement language; removing item #5-Police Ammunition Purchase Project in the 
amount of $40,000.00 and moving the funding in the amount $40,000.00 to item #13-Downtown 
Development Authority (DDA) District Improvements and Maintenance Project for a total of 
$6,017,966.00 and renumbering remaining sections. The substitute also includes the following 
amendments that were discussed but not amended during the meeting:  Language revisions to item 
#8-Municipal Building Space Need Analysis Plan Project to reflect inclusion of design and 
engineering elements and creation of a fund for other needs at the current municipal building if 
funds are available; language revisions to item #7-Johnson Pool Renovation Project to reflect 
inclusion of design and engineering elements and determination of renovation or replacement 
costs, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  It was noted that the road maintenance project and airport parking 
lot pavement project were discussed at the April 14th Committee of the Whole meeting as 
standalone projects on the ballot.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body.  County Commissioner Gunnar Malm, upon request, provided information on the 
upcoming April 28th meeting and explained it would be on Zoom and open to the public for limited 
number of attendees, noting members of the 1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax Committee 
will also be in attendance to start going through the projects.  Mr. Malm explained there will be 3 
bundle types for the projects and they will be put together in likeliness of projects and that a draft 
of projects would be presented at the meeting for consideration and discussion by the committee.  
He further noted there will be consideration for the City and Laramie County for standalone road 

   
projects and that some Laramie County fire projects could be paired with the City’s fire projects.  
Commissioner Malm, upon inquiry, advised that although the state statute has been revised to 
decrease the number of rural communities needed to approve the projects, the goal is for all towns 
to support approval.  He reported the committee wants to be as inclusive as possible and work as 
a team as the county consists of 100,000 people.  Comment was made in support of inclusion of 
all affected community members.  Main motion as amended carried.   Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”. (#6140)

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 22, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:07 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Ken Esquibel, Pete 
Laybourn, Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Also 
attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda. Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received 
by Mr. Case and Mr. Shanor, agenda item #22 (a) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon 
request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Cook and Mr. Roybal, agenda item 
#22 (b) was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Luna moved to approve remaining items, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on June 8, 2020.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Special Meetings of the Governing Body on May 28, June 11, June 15, and 
June 16,2020.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
ORDINANCE – 3rd READING – Granting a Non-Exclusive Franchise to Union Telephone 
Company within the rights-of-way of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Reporting for Finance 
Committee, Mr. Luna moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#4315) 
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending Section 2.76.020, Members-Terms-
Vacancies, of Chapter 2.76, Downtown Development Authority, of Title 2, Administration and 
Personnel, of the Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Amber Ash, Executive 
Director of the Downtown Development Authority, advised she was available for questions.  Upon 
inquiry, Ms. Ash explained that the DDA Board received applications from three qualified 
members, and with only two available seats open on the Board, they are requesting to expand the 
number of members from 9 to 10.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Case moved to 
approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Luna.  Comment was made on the need to expand 
the membership based on submitted qualified applications and the history of board member 
uncompleted terms.  Ms. Ash reported that per State Statute, the board can have a total of 11 
members.   Mayor Orr cautioned having an even numbered membership.  Ms. Ash noted that items 

  
considered for approval would require 6 votes to pass.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “no”.   
 
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LR (Low-Density Residential) to MUR (Mixed-
Use Residential Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of Kelley 
Drive and Old Faithful Road.  Tom Jacobson, #1 Properties, owner representative, requested 
consideration for approval.  Kari Happold, Rustic Wyoming Reality, advised they will be 
purchasing the office space for use by the real estate company and are requesting approval which 
will allow them to expand the business.  Mark Christensen, Planning and Development 
Department, advised he was available to answer questions.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second 
reading, seconded by Mr. Esquibel.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body. 
     
ORDINANCE – 2nd READING – Amending the official zoning map of the City of 
Cheyenne changing the zoning classification from LI (Light Industrial) to MUB (Mixed-Use 
Business Emphasis) for property generally located northeast of the intersection of west 21st Street 
and Reed Avenue.   It was noted that Matt Ashby, representative for the property owner, was 
available for any questions.  Mr. Case moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

Transfer of retail liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Big D Oil 
Co., d/b/a Big D Oil #29, 100 North Greeley Hwy., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held 
by Appaloosa Enterprises LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub).  Mr. Luna moved to approve, 
seconded by Mr. White.  Comment was made related to retail liquor licenses being utilized by 
convenience stores and how it is the least effective use of that type of license. As there are currently 
no available retail liquor licenses, they are an asset to the city and should be utilized in the best 
way possible to promote economic development.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Esquibel, 
Mr. Laybourn, Mr. Luna, Mayor Orr, Mr. Shanor and Mr. White.  Voting “no” – Mr. Case, Mr. 
Cook, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Roybal. 
 
 
Bar and grill liquor license application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Appaloosa 
Enterprises, LLC d/b/a Sanford’s Grub & Pub, 115 E. 17th St., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Clyde Mead, 
Big D Oil, speaking on the previous agenda item (transfer of retail liquor license application) 
provided information relating to staff training relating to meeting state and local liquor licensing 
regulations and reported customers have requested the service at the south Big D Oil location.  Mr. 
Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body.          
 
 
No comments were made on the Voucher Report as presented. 
 
 
No comments were made on the Revenue Report as presented. 
 
Reappointments of Jordan Evans, Terry Roker, Jennifer Lundberg and Barbara Fecht to 
the Public Transit Advisory Board; Lieutenant Eric Kent, Lieutenant David Janes, Kathy Emmons, 
Kimberly Skoutary-Johnson and Carla Thurin to the Laramie County Juvenile Service Joint 

  
Powers Board; Dr. Jasper James Chen to the Cheyenne/Laramie County Economic Development 
Joint Powers Board; Barry Sims, Tyler McLachlan and Tony O’Brien to the Tourism Promotion 
Joint Powers Board. Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  
Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not 
vote (mayoral appointments). 
 
Appointments of Kristina Richardson and Molly Palmer to the Mayor’s Council for People 
with Disabilities.  Mr. Luna moved to approve, seconded Mr. Roybal.  Motion carried.  Voting 
“yes” - all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Orr who did not vote 
(mayoral appointments). 
 
 
Under other business: 
 
 
Mr. White referenced a recent letter to the editor in the Wyoming Tribune Eagle that was 
complementary of work performed by the City Forestry crews and arborists that were sent to 
Laramie to help with tree issues after a damaging snow storm.  Mr. White expressed appreciation 
to the DDA, Thankful Thursdays and Lynn Buys Houses for the recent ‘Summertime on the 
Streets’ event, noting he is unaware of any open container issues associated with the event and that 
downtown businesses were appreciative for additional business.  Mayor Orr echoed Mr. White’s 
comments regarding appreciation for the City’s Forestry crew.  
 
 
Mr. Esquibel advised he had been contacted by citizens who expressed concern relating to 
a recent Police and Community Together meeting who feel forums of this nature should not be 
held at Police Headquarters and instead in a neutral location.  Mr. Esquibel advised a forum has 
been scheduled at the Laramie County Library on July 18th.  Up inquiry, Mr. Esquibel advised no 
law enforcement members will be present, elected officials will attend by invitation only, it will 
be open to the public and all panelists have been predetermined.  Mayor Orr reported the local 
Chapter of NAACP will meet on Saturday, June 27th from 10:00 a.m. to Noon at Element Church 
to allow for marginalized citizens voices to be heard.  
 
 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was 
officially adjourned at 6:43 p.m. 
 
 
 
Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
July 2, 2020

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_59048238.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
June 29, 2020 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met a special meeting on this date beginning 
at 6:27 p.m. via electronic conference meeting.  Attending electronic conference meeting were:  
MAYOR – Marian J. Orr; COUNCIL MEMBERS – Rocky Case, Bryan Cook, Pete Laybourn, 
Mike Luna, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Dicky Shanor and Jeff White.  Absent-Ken Esquibel.  
Also attending: Kris Jones, City Clerk.    
 
RESOLUTION – Providing for the City of Cheyenne to apply for “CARES ACT” and 
other federally available funds to address the COVID-19 Pandemic.  (Sponsor – Dr. Rinne) Mr. 
Shanor abstained from voting due to personal employment affiliation with the Wyoming State 
Loan and Investment Board.  Mr. Roybal moved to suspend the rules pursuant to State Statute §15-
1-115 to consider the resolution for final adoption, seconded by Mr. Case.  Motion carried.   Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor who didn’t 
vote.  Mr. Case moved to amend by adding the following language: “All applications to any entity 
shall be aligned to the CARES Act spending priorities passed as part of the City’s FY2021 budget 
ordinance”, seconded by Mr. Luna. Michael O’Donnell, City Attorney, expressed concern and 
urged caution in approving the proposed amendment relating to annual budget requirements and 
proposed grant funding regulations.  Mr. Case with concurrence from Mr. Luna, withdrew the 
proposed amendment.  Mr. White moved to amend by adding the following language in the Now 
Therefore Be It Resolved clause following the word pandemic “in an amount not to exceed five 
million dollars”, seconded by Mr. Cook.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Shanor who didn’t vote. Main motion as 
amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Shanor who didn’t vote. (#6070) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Encouraging summer events at the Cheyenne Depot Plaza.   (Sponsors – 
Mr. Case, Mr. Cook, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Luna, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Roybal, Mr. Shanor & Mr. White) – 
referred to Public Services Committee. 
       
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held July 13, 2020 at 6:00 p.m. via 
electronic conference meeting, to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required 
by Wyoming Statute §15-1-402 exist for Lots 1 and 2 and a Portion of a 20 Foot Vacated Alley 
being adjacent to and West of said Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Prairie Hills Addition, Laramie County, 
Wyoming (located northwest of the intersection of Yellowstone Road and Western Hills Blvd).  
 
 
 
Mayor Orr stated the purpose of the special meeting had been met, and there being no 
further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially adjourned at 6:37 
p.m. 
 
 
 
 
 

Submitted by, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request. 
 
Published: 
Wyoming Tribune-Eagle 
 
 
July 10, 2020

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM CB 
COMMUNITY BUSINESS TO MUB MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS FOR A 
PORTION OF THE SE¼ OF SECTION 33, T.14N., R.66W., 6TH P.M., 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED SOUTH OF AND ADJACENT TO SPARKS 
ROAD, WEST OF RIDGE ROAD).
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map 
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on June 15, 2020, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as a portion of the SE¼ of 
Section 33, T.14N., R.66W., 6th P.M., Cheyenne, Wyoming (more particularly described as follows: 
Commencing at the Southeast corner of said Section 33, being a found aluminum cap monument; thence 
N0°07'31"E along the east line of said Section 33, 1957.51 feet to a set 1½" aluminum cap marked IME, 
PLS 5134, herein after referred to as a typical monument, at the southwest corner of Block 2, McCann 
Addition and the POINT OF BEGINNING of this parcel description; thence from said Point of Beginning 
continue N0°07'31"E along said east line 562.11 feet to a found aluminum cap monument on the south 
right-of-way line of East Lincolnway; thence westerly along said southerly right-of-way line on an arc of 
a non-tangent curve to the right 407.62 feet to a typical monument, said curve having a radius of 3969.83 
feet, central angle of 5°52'59" and chord bearing S87°12'51"W, 407.44 feet; thence N89°44'15"W along 
said southerly right-of-way line, 141.96 feet to a found aluminum cap monument at the northeast corner 
of Lot 2, Block 1, Physician's Medical Park Subdivision; thence S0°06'42"W along the east line of said 
Physicians Medical Park Subdivision, 672.92 feet to a found aluminum cap monument; thence 
S45°32'33"W along the east line of said Physicians Medical Park Subdivision, 74.23 feet to a found 
aluminum cap monument at the southeast corner of Lot 3, Block 1, of said Physicians Medical Park 
Subdivision being on the northeasterly right-of-way line of Henderson Drive; thence S44°33'04"E along 
said northeasterly right-of-way line, 256.08 feet to a found iron pipe on the northwesterly right-of-way 
line of Nationway Highway; thence northeasterly along said northwesterly right-of-way line on an arc of 
a non-tangent curve to the left, 15.21 feet, said curve having a radius of 3669.73 feet, central angle of 
0°14'15", and chord bearing N50°30'25"E, 15.21 feet; thence N49°10'18"E along said northwesterly right-
of-way line, 542.64 feet to the Point of Beginning, containing 9.60 acres, more or less) is hereby changed 
to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication,
and the recording of the annexation encompassing the area described in Section 4 with the County Clerk 
and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
3 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning of unplatted, undeveloped land between 
Lincolnway and Nationway from CB Community Business to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis. This 
property is located west of Ridge Road. The specific proposal associated with this application is to 
develop a portion of the property with a multi-family use. Multi-family is prohibited in the current CB 
zone. The remainder of the property has no specific development proposals at this time, but zoning to 
MUB would allow either residential or commercial uses in the future.  
 
The current Future Land Use designation for the property is Community Business, and the optimal zone 
for that designation is the current CB Community Business zone. However, a petition to amend the Future 
Land Use Map has been submitted and is being processed concurrently with this application. The 
proposed designation is Mixed-use Commercial, and the MUB zone is the suggested zone for the Mixed-
use Commercial designation. Principles and policies in PlanCheyenne encourage MUB zoning at this 
location. These policies encourage increasing housing options in the community and higher-density 
housing near transportation corridors and commercial activity centers. By zoning this property MUB, 
higher-density housing would be allowed in this area of the community.  
 
Due to the surrounding development patterns, there is limited opportunity for this project to provide the 
highly connected, smaller block pattern encouraged for areas zoned MUB. However, there may be an 
opportunity to create a connection between Lincolnway and Nationway. 
 
The proposed MUB zoning is compatible with the surrounding area considering the existing zoning and 
the comprehensive plan’s policies for the area. The proposed MUB zoning allows many of the same 
commercial uses as the nearby CB zoning. MUB zoning also allows for multifamily residential development 
– which is an anticipated component of activity centers according to the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria of Section 2.2.1.d of the Cheyenne Unified Development 
Code, staff recommends approval of this zone change. 
 
 

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
4 
 
LOCATION/VICINITY MAP:  
 
 
 
 
Map # 1 – Project Location/ Vicinity Map  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS Cooperative Program 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Project Location 

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
5 
 
AERIAL OF SUBJECT PROPERTY: 
 
 
 
 
VIEWS OF SUBJECT PROPERTY: 
 
 
Map # 2 - Site aerial highlighting subject property located 
between Lincolnway and Nationway, west of Ridge Road.  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS Cooperative Program 
Photograph #1 – Image of the subject property from

from the intersection Nationway and Mulberry

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
6 
 
ZONE CHANGE DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions 
on a proposed Zone Change Request, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following criteria: 
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical 
patterns identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  
 
PlanCheyenne is partially supportive of this zone 
change.  
 
The future land use map currently identifies the 
subject property, and most of the surrounding 
parcels, as Community Business. The Community 
Business designation is expected to primarily 
develop with regional serving retail and office 
uses. Service uses are also appropriate. 
Secondary uses include open space, recreation, 
civic, and multifamily residential. 
 
While the current CB Community Business zone is 
considered the optimal zone for this future land 
use designation, it does not allow for the 
secondary use of multi-family residential. The 
proposed MUB zone still allows a range of 
commercial uses – including the retail, office, and 
service uses considered optimal by the future land 
use map designation. However, the MUB zone would also allow a broader range of residential 
uses than just the multi-family considered by the current future land use designation. 
 
Principles and policies within the Comprehensive Plan support the zone change. Principle 1.1 
encourages a balanced mix of uses and flexibility to respond to market demands – noting that 
the Future Land Use Map is a guide, not a predeterminer of specific uses or zones. Policy 1.3.b 
encourages a mix of housing types, densities, and unit sizes. Policy 2.3.b encourages higher 
density and senior housing near or within activity centers and adjacent to transportation 
corridors, and MUB zoning at this location would allow for higher-density housing in this area of 
the community, which is near major transportation corridors and an activity center. Principle 2.5 
encourages the provision of housing throughout the community, and by changing the zoning to 
MUB at this location, more locations for potential housing development are provided. 
 
A concurrent petition has been submitted requesting an amendment the Future Land Use Map for 
the subject property, changing the future land use designation to Mixed-use Commercial. The 
optimal zoning for the proposed future land use designation is the MUB zone. If the Future Land 
Use Map amendment is approved, then the MUB zoning of this property is highly compatible with 
PlanCheyenne. 
 
 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
7 
 
2. SURROUNDING AREA - The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering 
existing zoning, the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the 
guidance of that plan for these areas.  
 
The proposed MUB zoning is compatible with the 
surrounding area considering the existing zoning and 
the comprehensive plan’s policies for the area. The 
proposed MUB zoning allows many of the same 
commercial uses as the CB zoning (although more 
intense uses may be subject to a higher standard of 
review). MUB zoning also allows for multifamily 
residential development, which is an anticipated 
component of commercial activity centers according 
to the comprehensive plan.  
 
The properties to the east and west of this proposed 
zone change are currently zoned CB Community 
Business and are largely developed with Community 
Business – style development patterns. The current 
CB zoning links these developed properties and 
creates a large swath of CB zoning, though this 
specific parcel has not yet been developed. 
 
Land to the north of this proposed zone change is zoned LR Low-density Residential and 
developed with residential and assembly (church) uses. Land to the southeast is also developed 
with a residential neighborhood (with MR Medium-density Residential and P Public zoning) – but 
both of these residential neighborhoods are separated from the subject property by wide rights-
of-way. To the southwest, another largely residential neighborhood is separated from the subject 
property by a drainage swale.   
 
The current Future Land Use Map designates this area as Community Business and anticipates 
largely commercial uses and zoning. However, policies within the plan support the proposed zone 
change and an amendment to the Future Land Use Map is proposed for the subject property. 
 
 
3. ZONING: BASIC STANDARDS & USES - The suitability of the subject property for development 
according to the standards and uses of the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the 
subject property for development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning 
district.  
 
The current CB Community Business zoning allows a wide variety of commercial uses, but does 
not allow any residential uses. The CB zone generally has a lower level of design standards than 
MUB zoning. The MUB zone allows a broader mix of uses, including most types of residential 
uses, but is a little more limiting on intensity of commercial uses.  The proposed MUB zoning also 
encourages structures to be built closer to the street with higher design standards. 
Map #4 – Zone Districts 
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
8 
 
4. STREETS & OPEN SPACE - The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed 
according to the Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning.  
 
The MUB zoning is intended to have a highly connected street network with smaller blocks. Due 
to the surrounding development patterns, there is limited opportunity for this project to provide 
the block pattern encouraged for areas zoned MUB. However, there may be an opportunity to 
create a connection between Lincolnway and Nationway at this site. The applicant is seeking to 
rezone the land in conjunction with a platting action. The proposed plat would be analyzed for 
compliance with subdivision standards. 
 
 
5. ZONING: INTENT, APPLICABILITY, & DESIGN OBJECTIVES - The ability of the application to support the 
Intent and Applicability statements of the proposed zoning district, and achieve the Design Objectives 
of any specific standards. 
 
The MUB zone is intended to have a diverse mix of uses in a compact development pattern. Uses 
are intended to be integrated with smaller, walkable blocks. The MUB zone is also intended as an 
alternative to CB zoning for a commercial activity center. The subject property is surrounded by 
development and has limited opportunity to create the highly-connected, smaller blocks 
encouraged by MUB zoning. However, there is an opportunity to provide connection between 
Lincolnway and Nationway though this site.    
 
 
6. ZONING: FUTURE DEVELOPMENT - The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable 
standards for development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options in 
the event that any specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed 
after approval.  
 
The specific development associated with this proposed zone change is a multi-family 
development on a small portion of the property. Future development and uses of a majority of 
the property have yet to be determined. The applicant is seeking the rezoning for the entire 
property with the expectation of developing other portions of the property in the future. Such 
future development could include either commercial or residential components, and the proposed 
zoning of MUB allows either option. Should the specifically-proposed development not be 
executed, future development on that portion of the parcel would have a similar variety of use 
options. 
 
 
7. REVIEW – The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. 
  
The following agencies were assigned to review this proposal: Community Recreation and Events, 
Engineering, Metropolitan Planning Organization, Planning and Development, and the Wyoming 
Department of Transportation.  
 
 
 

Zone Change Request  |  UDC-20-00155 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
9 
Reviewing Agencies with Comments: 
- 
Metropolitan Planning Organization 
o 
(FYI) In future development of this plat, it would be nice to remove the partial service 
road along Nationway. 
o 
(FYI) The future roadway through this development can possibly tie to the intersection 
of Mulberry Ave & Nationway. 
o 
(FYI) There is a need to see curb, gutter and sidewalk along Sparks Rd and sidewalks to 
connect between current site and connections to future sites. 
- 
Planning and Development 
o 
(FYI) Zone district is CB, with no MUB in the near vicinity. There is existing residential 
uses adjacent along the southwesterly and southeasterly boundary. Multi-family may be 
a good transition between existing commercial and residential uses. 
o 
(FYI) This zone change is accompanied by an update to the Future Land Use Map in 
PlanCheyenne. If the update to the Future Land Use Map is adopted, the zone change 
will be in conformance with the Comprehensive Plan. Staff is supportive of the update to 
the Future Land Use Map to accommodate this zone change. The zone change has been 
requested to permit the construction of multi-family housing and a mix of uses on an 
infill development site, accomplishing several goals within PlanCheyenne. 
o 
(FYI) MUB zoning for this property is only partially supported by the Future Land Use 
Map. The Community Business future land use designation does consider multi-family 
residential as an acceptable secondary use, and the current CB zoning would not allow 
residential uses. However, the Comprehensive Plan does have principles and policies 
which support amending the map and the proposed zone change (ref: 1.1.B, 1.3.b, 
2.3.b, 2.5.a, and the comparison of the description of the Mixed-use Commercial 
Emphasis versus the description of the Community Business Future Land Use 
designations). 
o 
(FYI) The subject property meets the intent and applicability of both the CB and MUB 
zones. 
o 
(FYI) The land is currently vacant and unplatted - lot types would be determined at 
platting action. 
o 
(FYI) MUB zoning requires new non-residential development to meet design standards 
of 6.7. 
All other reviewing agencies did not provide Comments. 
 
Other Comments Received: 
- 
None. 
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public health, 
safety, or general welfare.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_85450421.pdf
Approved as to
ORDINANCE NO.
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTER 10.52, STOPPING, STANDING
AND PARKING GENERALLY, OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC,
OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1. That the following section of Chapter 10.52, Stopping, Standing and Parking
Generally, ofTitle 10, Vehicles and Traffic, is hereby amended as set out in this ordinance. Sections
currently existing in Chapter 10.52 not set out within this ordinance remain unchanged.
10.52.010
Method of parking.
A.
No person shall stand or park a vehicle in the roadway other than parallel with the
edge ofthe roadway, headed in the direction oflawful traffic movement, and with the wheels ofthe
vehicle nearest the curbline on the roadway but within twelve (12) inches ofthe curb or edge ofthe
roadway, except two- or three-wheeled motor vehicles and vehicles standing or parked in a cul-de
sac, which may park at an angle to the curb and abutting the curb, provided they do not obstruct
traffic movement and except as otherwise provided in this title.
B.
No person shall be permitted to back a motor vehicle into a diagonal parking space
within city limits.
Section 2. This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication.

THIRD AND FINAL READING:___________________

Publish: Wyoming Tribune Eagle

APPROVED
LBY Alessandra at 1:52 pm, Jul 07, 2020J
ORDINANCE NO.
ENTITLED: “ANNEXING
TO
THE
CITY
OF
CHEYENNE,
WYOMING,
LAND
LOCATED NORTHWEST OF THE INTERSECTION OF WESTERN
HILLS BOULEVARD AND YELLOWSTONE ROAD.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1.
That the City of Cheyenne hereby finds as follows:
(a)
That Jeff Wallace and Ed Murray, owners of Lots 1 and 2 and a portion of a 20
foot vacated alley being adjacent to and west of said Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Prairie
Hills Addition, Laramie County, Wyoming, have petitioned the governing body
of the City of Cheyenne to annex the real property described herein and to make it
a part thereof; and
(b)
That an accurate annexation map of said land is attached to this ordinance as
Exhibit A, said map prepared by Adam
F. Deschler, a Professional Land
Surveyor, licensed in the State of Wyoming, PLS Number 12045; and
(c)
That the annexation of said area is for the protection of health, safety, and welfare
of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and
(d)
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and
(e)
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason,
be furnished; and
(0
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned MU
Mixed-Use by the Laramie County Commissioners; and
(g)
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and
its governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area
pursuant to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the
entire annexed area.
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer’s Office to add said area to
the official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.
Section 4.
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared
illegal or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the
remaining phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.
Section 5.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:
This annexation application is for approximately .75 acres of land near the intersection of Western Hills
Boulevard and Yellowstone Road. This application was submitted along with a zone change application.
Any land annexed into the City is required to be given a City zone district. The zone change application
proposes changing the zoning from County MU Mixed-Use to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis.
PlanCheyenne is supportive of annexing this land into the City. This land is located within the Urban
Service Boundary and is designated in the future land use plan as Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis.
Furthermore, the proposed annexation is currently a County enclave, meaning it is entirely surrounded by
City limits. City services will provide environmental quality benefits to both the current and future
residents.
As with any expansion there will be an impact on service provision within the City. However, as this is a
small expansion adjacent to current City Services, the impact on City services will be minor. The property
is surrounded by City limits on all sides.
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute 15-1-402, staffrecommends
approval of this annexation.

ANNEXATION ELIGIBILITY DATA/ANALYSIS: Before any territory is eligible for annexation, the
governing body must find that the following criteria are met (W.S. § 15-1-402 (a)):
COMMON WELFARE
— An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety, and we/fare of
the persons residing in the area and in the city or town.
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of current and any
future residents or tenants of the subject property. Residents and businesses will have full access
to City services once the annexation is completed without detriment to surrounding property
owners.
PART OF THE Cn-y
— The urban development of the area sought to be annexed wou/d be constituted a
natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the annexing city or town.
This property will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the city. The
property is surrounded by developed land within City boundaries. The subject properties are
considered a County enclave as they are surrounded by City limits. This annexation will constitute
a logical expansion of the City by removing a County island. Furthermore, any future residents or
tenants of the subject property will be seamlessly integrated into the economic and social parts
of the community with the office park to the North and surrounding homes and businesses to the
South, East, and West.
SERVICE EXTENSION LOGICAL EXPANSION
— The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible
addition to the annexing city or town and the extension ofbasic and other services customarily available
to residents of the city or town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed.
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City
services normally available to residents will also be available. Police and fire protection will be
extended upon annexation. Connection to City water and sewer lines is possible upon annexation
and application to the Board of Public Utilities.
CONTIGUOUS
— The area sought to be annexed is conti’uous with
or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or the area meets the
requirements of
WS. 15-1-407.
The properties that are proposed for annexation have a
perimeter that is 100% contiguous with City limits.
Contiguity is achieved along all sides.
City Limits
Map #4
— Current City Boundaries
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS
Cooperative Program
Annexation/UDC-20-0023 1
7

UTILITIES
— If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to service the annexed area pursuant to W. S.
15-1-410.
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.
CERTIFIED MAILING
— The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the
public hearing required by W5. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mall to all landowners and affected
public utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date, and location of the public hearing required by
WS. 15-1-405(a).
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on June 12, 2020, and the
earliest confirmation of receipt was recorded on June 13, 2020. The public hearing for this
annexation is scheduled for July 13, 2018. Twenty (20) business days prior to the announced
public hearing is June 12, 2020.
ANNEXATION REPORT DATA: In compliance with W.S. § 15-1-402(c) the following information is
provided:
(i)
A map is not required for this report based on the absence of exercising the authority
granted under W.S. 15-3-202 (b) (ii).
(ii)
The total estimated cost of infrastructure improvements required of the landowners by the
City related to the annexation is approximately $3500 per household. Estimated costs include
the connection of existing structures to sanitary sewer. Additional on- and off-site
improvements may be required with subsequent development actions.
(iii)
Services customarily available to City residents include water, sewer, sanitation, police and
fire protection. Police and Fire services will be provided immediately upon the recording of
the annexation. Water and sewer services are provided upon application and payment to the
Board of Public Utilities, with sanitation services occurring concurrently. Provision of these
services is initiated by the property owner. Development will require extension of water and
sewer services, which are available to the subject property.
(iv)
Fees for water, sewer, and sanitation services are a variable amount for residential units,
based upon volume and meter size. For single-family residential lots, total annual fees,
including water, sewer and sanitation services, are estimated at $1,200.
(v)
The current property tax mill levy imposed is 67.20 and projected property tax mill levy
imposed by the city is 72.20.
(vi)
The infrastructure described in (ii) above includes improvements required within the existing
boundaries of the municipality

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_18992877.pdf
I APPROVED
LBY Alessandra at 1:50 pm, Jul07, 2020
ORDINANCE NO.
_______________
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY
OF
CHEYENNE
CHANGING
THE
ZONING
CLASSIFICATION
FROM
COUNTY MU (MIXED-USE) TO MUB (MIXED-USE BUSINESS EMPHASIS)
FOR
PROPERTY
GENERALLY
LOCATED
NORTHWEST
OF
THE
INTERSECTION OF WESTERN HILLS BOULEVARD AND YELLOWSTONE
ROAD.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein has (have) applied for a zoning map
amendment (“zone change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment,
of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).
Section 2. That on July 6, 2020, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended
approval of said zone change.
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC.
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lots
1 and 2 and a
portion of a 20 foot vacated alley being adjacent to and west of said Lots 1 and 2, Block I, Prairie Hills
Addition, Cheyenne, Wyoming is hereby changed to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis.
Section 5. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 7 below.
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and
phrases.
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication,
and the recording of the annexation encompassing the area described in Section 4 with the County Clerk
and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning of Lots 1 and 2 and a portion of a 20-foot
vacated alley being adjacent to and West of said Lots 1 and 2 of Block 1 of the Prairie Hills Addition from
County MU Mixed-Use to MUB Mixed-Use Business Emphasis. The area is proposed to be annexed to the
City of Cheyenne and therefore requires a City zone. The subject properties are proposed to be annexed
to connect to City services provided by the Board of Public Utilities.
The future land use map designates the subject properties and surrounding area as Mixed-use
Commercial Emphasis. This category includes a wide variety of land uses and activities, focused primarily
on commercial activities, but also including other employment, public, and residential uses. The optimal
zone for the Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis future land use category is MUB Mixed-Use Business
Emphasis. Furthermore, the MUB zoning designation conforms to the zoning of the surrounding
properties within the incorporated City.
The current uses of the subject properties are residential and office. There is no proposed new
development or redevelopment at this time.
Due to meeting the review criteria ofSection 2.2.1.d ofthe Cheyenne Unified Development
Code, staffrecommends approval ofthis zone change.

Emphasis, will allow for the broad mix of uses

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_47867831.pdf
1 
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A 
RESOLUTION 
CREATING 
A 
SPECIAL 
DISTRICT 
FOR 
‘CHEYENNE DAYS – LEGENDARY NIGHTS EVENTS’, JULY 17, 2020 
TO JULY 26, 2020, IN WHICH THE POSSESSION OF OPEN 
CONTAINERS IS PERMITTED WITHIN THE DOWNTOWN DISTRICT 
BOUNDARIES.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body has amended Section 5.12.110 to 
allow for creation of special districts in which the possession of open containers is permitted; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) requests the boundaries of 
their district be declared a special district for the “Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights Event” at 
the following dates and times: 
 
1. Paint Slingers 2020 – Saturday, July 18, 2020, from 12:00 p.m. to 9:30 p.m. (alcohol 
serving time – actual event times will be from 10:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.)  
 
2. Cheyenne Day – Wednesday, July 22, 2020, from 10:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m. 
 
3. Fridays on the Plaza – Friday, July 24, 2020, from 5:30 p.m. to 9:00 p.m. 
 
4. Saturday, July 25, 2020, from 7:00 a.m. to 10:00 p.m.; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne Frontier Days is an economic engine for Laramie County that 
generates approximately $36.2 million in business activity between visitor spending and the 
creation of jobs, generating approximately $1.2 million in local and state sales tax revenue; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, in the interest of public health and safety, the 2020 Cheyenne Frontier Days 
has been cancelled due to the coronavirus pandemic; and 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne Days – Legendary Nights Events will promote local shopping by 
offering shopping specials, sidewalk sales, and entertainment including a street dance, concerts, 
potentially a pancake breakfast, and the Thunderbirds Air Show; and  
 
WHEREAS, shopping locally creates a multiplier effect through direct, indirect, and 
induced spending which boosts the local economy and creates more local wealth and jobs; and 
 
WHERAS, a number of studies illustrated on the website of American Independence 
Business Alliance (AMIBA) have proven a 48% return to the community occurs when you spend 
locally; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the event organizers will comply with any and all laws, rules, and 
regulations to ensure the safety of the community; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, adherence to the following requirements are required for participation by 
alcohol vendors and consumers when open containers are permitted as designated herein: 
 
1. Wristbands shall be awarded by the licensee only to a person 21 years of age or more and 
must be worn and visible to purchase, possess, or consume liquor within the designated 
special district downtown area.  The wristbands shall have an identifying mark, name, or 
logo, as required by the City. 
 
2. The only containers for alcoholic beverages in the special district downtown area’s public 
spaces, for the day specified herein, are designated plastic cups.  The cups shall have an 
identifying mark, name, or logo, as required by the City. 
 
3. Beer and malt beverages (including malt beverage alternatives shall be served in 12 oz. 
cups; servings of wine shall not exceed a 5 oz. serving; and mixed drinks, whose mixed 
contents shall contain no more than 1.5 oz. of distilled spirit(s) per serving. 
By Alessandra at 3:42 pm, Jun 18, 2020

2 
 
 
4. No outside alcoholic beverage, wine, or malt liquor may be brought into or consumed 
within the open container area; a violation of this provision is a violation of the City’s 
open container prohibition and potentially other state or local statutes or codes. 
 
5. Nothing herein shall be interpreted as authorizing the possession of open containers of 
alcoholic liquor or malt beverages in or on motor vehicles. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the boundaries of the Downtown Development 
Authority district are declared a special district for the purposes of hosting the “Cheyenne Days – 
Legendary Nights Event” in which the possession of open containers will be permitted during the 
dates and times designated herein. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ______ day of ________________, 2020. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
 
Attest: 
 
__________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

RESOLUTION NO. _________ 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION ENCOURAGING SUMMER EVENTS AT THE 
CHEYENNE DEPOT PLAZA.” 
WHEREAS, city staff developed a plan to have Friday’s on the Plaza events that met 
all guidelines established by the State of Wyoming and the Cheyenne-Laramie County 
Health Department put in place due to COVID-19; and 
WHEREAS, the plan was submitted to and approved by officials at the Cheyenne-
Laramie County Health Department; and 
WHEREAS, Friday’s on the Plaza events provide economic benefits to downtown 
businesses and the City of Cheyenne; and 
WHEREAS, Friday’s on the Plaza events were scheduled to begin on July 10; and 
WHEREAS, the Mayor made the decision to cancel these important community 
events without input from members of the Governing Body or officials at the 
Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Department; and 
WHEREAS, local and state health officials did not recommend or advise cancellation 
of these events; and 
WHEREAS, this unilateral decision by the Mayor will cause irreparable economic 
damage to downtown businesses and the City of Cheyenne. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AS FOLLOWS: 
1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne respectfully asks the Mayor 
to reconsider her decision to cancel Friday’s on the Plaza events unless 
otherwise recommended by health officials from the Cheyenne-Laramie 
County Health Department and the State of Wyoming.  
2. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne supports and encourages 
events to be held at the Cheyenne Depot Plaza provided they meet all 
guidelines and recommendations from the Cheyenne-Laramie County Health 
Department and the State of Wyoming that have been put in place due to 
COVID-19. 

3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne hopes that future decisions 
will be made in concert with local and state health officials and members of the 
Governing Body.  
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this ______ day of _________________, 
2020. 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
_________________________________________ 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
ATTEST: 
___________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 


--- END OF FILE ---

RESOLUTION NO. _____________ 
 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING THE MUNICIPAL COMPLEX 
PEDESTRIAN ROUTING PLAN.” 
 
WHEREAS, the public space studied is between 20th Street to the south, O’Neil Avenue 
to the west, 22nd Street on the north and Pioneer Avenue to the east; and 
 
WHEREAS, the public space in the interior of the “super block” of the City Municipal 
Building, Civic Center and Cox Parking Garage was originally developed in 1979, is not well lit, 
and has outdated pedestrian facilities with obstructions and maintenance issues; and 
 
WHEREAS, there have been numerous civic improvements in the vicinity including the 
Laramie County Library, St. Mary’s School, expansion of the County Jail, Civic Commons and 
growing events at the Civic Center; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization (MPO) retained Russell 
+ Mills Studios on August 8, 2018 to develop the Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan; 
and 
WHEREAS, goals for the Plan are to enhance connections within the Municipal Complex, 
increase overall pedestrian safety, comfort and function, and to continue to establish Municipal 
Complex character and identity; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Plan reviews and makes recommendations for the Municipal Building’s 
North Parking Lot and provided parking for the adjacent private property owned by the Cheyenne 
Housing Authority; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Plan reviews and mads recommendations concerning the three existing 
memorials and envisioned them becoming more unified and placed together in a prominent 
location; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne MPO Technical and Citizens’ Advisory Committees reviewed 
the Municipal Complex Pedestrian Plan and recommended approval by the MPO Policy 
Committee; and 
 
WHEREAS, public involvement for the project consisted of an open house on October 23, 
2018, two web-based surveys, Civic Center Lobby displays, and the City of Cheyenne Planning 
Commission public meeting on June 15, 2020 where public comments were accepted, and the 
Planning Commission recommended that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body approve and 
certify the Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan. 
 
 
 
 
 
By Alessandra at 11:15 am, Jun 30, 2020

NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
THAT, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby certifies the “Municipal Complex 
Pedestrian Routing Plan” dated June 2020. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the “Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan” 
will be used as guidance for the future design and reconstruction of the Municipal Complex Public 
Space once project prioritization is advanced and funding is secured.  
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS ______ DAY OF _____________, 2020. 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
Marian J. Orr, Mayor

-1-
Civic Uses
Senior 
Activity  
Center
Parking
Pedestrian 
Connections 
and Safety
The Municipal Complex 
Pedestrian Routing Plan seeks 
to incorporate the needs of the 
above uses in a cohesive and 
comprehensive manner.
Project Vision
The City of Cheyenne is evolving and the Municipal Complex area is no different. 
Over the past decade, many changes have occurred, resulting in new and exciting 
challenges. With the addition of St. Mary’s School, the Laramie County Library, 
Municipal Court, growing popularity of events at the Civic Center, expansion of the 
County Jail, Civic Commons, and growing business development, the Municipal 
Complex is becoming a focal point of activity. While these neighbors provide a 
welcome vibrancy, they also require more efficient utilization of public facilities.
 
The Pedestrian Routing Plan provides a vision for intuitive and safe connections 
through the municipal breezeway area and to destinations within the complex and 
beyond.  Creating a more attractive, safe and functional pedestrian environment will 
benefit users while increasing the efficiency of use of facilities like the Cox Parking 
Structure.  Currently, the breezeway experiences limited use, but with adjustments 
could function as a connector and a community gathering space, linking the Laramie 
County Library, City Facilities and County Buildings together.  
 
Enhanced parking and circulation facilitates greater utilization of the Cox Parking 
Structure with the conversion of the City’s West parking lot into the Civic Commons.  
Providing clear pedestrian routing through the breezeway area helps offset the 
impacts from the recent shift in parking patterns.  Updated lighting aids in linking 
adjacent parking lots, while increasing their viability for evening performances at the 
Civic Center.  Other design enhancements create a more efficient circulation for The 
Burke Senior Center, while adding more short-term parking spaces.  Landscape and 
urban design enhancements create the atmosphere of an urban park, which can be 
used by patrons of the Senior Center, City Employees and all visitors to the Municipal 
Complex.
 
In short, the City of Cheyenne has an opportunity to transform what is currently a 
sparingly-used area into a focal point of the Municipal Complex.  This axis of activity 
will define a bright and positive pedestrian crossroads for the City of Cheyenne. 
Planning Process
The Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan was initiated in October 2018 
through an advisory committee workshop to determine overall issues, needs and 
Goals/Objectives. This was followed by a public workshop where three options for 
parking reconfiguration and three alternative framework concept plans were presented 
for evaluation. A survey was included in this workshop and was posted on-line for 
additional feedback.
Following this, the project was on-hold while a program plan and concept design was 
developed for the Civic Center, in order to coordinate the Pedestrian Routing Plan with 
this effort. A preliminary plan was developed in coordination with the Civic Center Plan 
and reviewed with the advisory committee in the Fall of 2019. A survey was conducted 
on-line and through displays at the Civic Center to receive additional feedback on the 
preliminay plan and the feedback was incorporated into this plan.
Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan
Project Background

-2-
Civic spine connections
A primary objective of the plan is to develop connections beyond the Municipal 
Complex in order to facilitate connections to municipal facilities beyond, through the 
creation of a Civic Spine along Thomes Ave. The Civic Spine connects the Laramie 
County Library to the Municipal Complex, the Cheyenne Public Safety Center and 
Lincolnway, through intersection enhancements that faciltate pedestrian movement and 
identity as a corridor. East-west connections allow connectivity to St. Mary’s Catholic 
School, the Laramie County Courthouse, the Post Office and the State Museum, etc. 
Site analysis
A site analysis shown on the following pages describes site opportunities and 
challenges including key connections, potential open spaces, gathering areas and 
features. Key elements include the Civic Spine opportunity, connections to existing 
and proposed building entries, existing memorials and potential integrated locations. 
In addition, storm-water facilites, parking opportunites and existing trees are key 
considerations in this site planning effort. 
Goals and objectives
Goals and Objectives were developed for the project outlining key components of the 
plan that are emphasized. These were developed with and reviewed by the project 
Advisory Committee and during a public workshop. These include the following:
1.	Enhance connections within the Municipal Complex and beyond
2.	Increase overall pedestrian safety, comfort and function
3.	Continue to establish Municipal Complex character and identity
Program
A program was developed through feedback from the advisory committee and the 
public workshop. The program outlines elements of the plan that are deemed critical to 
its success and are outlined on page six.
Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan

-5-
Goal #1: 
Enhance Connections within 
the Municipal Complex and to 
Destinations Beyond
•	 Create clear and intuitive entry points and connections
•	 Create clear and intuitive connections to adjacent parking lots 
and structures
•	 Create simple and clear walkways that contribute to formal 
expression and character
•	 Provide clear wayfinding through strong pathways and origin to 
destination visual cues and signage
•	 Establish Municipal Spine along Thomes Avenue
Goal #2: 
Increase Overall Pedestrian 
Safety, Comfort and Function
•	 Reduce pedestrian and vehicular conflict
•	 Create a safe and inviting evening experience for employees and 
visitors
•	 Reduce presence of middle story planting and visual clutter
•	 Activate and bring life to under-utilized spaces and assets
Goal #3: 
Continue to Establish Municipal 
Complex Character and Identity
•	 Use materials of enduring quality and that communicate civic 
pride
•	 Take cues from regional influences and stakeholders input that 
highlight historic and valued assets

Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan
Program Matrix

-7-
Parking quantities
The diagram on the following page compiles the information presented in the Cheyenne 
Municipal Area Parking Study, from September, 2016. The report studied options for 
parallel parking on both sides as well as diagonal parking on one side of the street. This 
diagram shows the overall direction that parking should have in order to get the highest 
yields as well as avoid unnecessary reconfigurations of the streets.
Through public process the new Civic Commons maintained parallel parking with new 
corner bulb-outs. In order to keep consistency along O’Neil the remaining blocks should 
remain parallel. West 22nd did not yield more parking by changing to diagonal on one 
side. Four (4) spaces were added from the report by closing off the north parking lot 
entrance drive.
The east side of 20th Street has room to add diagonal parking in order to increase the 
parking count along this area.
Three options were developed for the north parking lot to understand parking yields and 
trade-offs for each as they impact circulation, bus parking proximity to The Burke Senior 
Center, integration of the Civic Spine and preservation or removal of the existing storage 
building.
Each option proposes removal of the existing median and turnaround along Thomes Ave. 
with emergency access and turn-around provided on The Burke Senior Center property. 
This allows for a more efficient use of the space for parking and pedestrian circulation.
North lot option 1
Option 1 yields a loss of four parking spaces from existing quantities and two bus parking 
spaces, assuming spaces conform to Unified Development Code (UDC) standards. It 
maintains the existing storage building and provides perpendicular parking adjacent to The 
Burke Senior Center. Bus parking is provided on the western side of the drive aisle adjacent 
to The Burke Senior Center. 
North lot option 2
Option 2 yields nineteen additional spaces beyond existing quantities, three bus parking 
spaces and removes the existing storage building, assuming it may be possible to relocate. 
Bus parking is not adjacent to The Burke Senior Center and the Thomes alignment is 
slightly offset to the west. 
North lot option 3 - Preferred
Option 3 yields twenty additional spaces beyond existing quantities, three bus parking 
spaces adjacent to The Burke Senior Center and removes the existing storage building. 
The Thomes access point is aligned with the access point to the Laramie County Library.

-12-
Framework Concepts
Three framework concepts are shown on the following pages outlining variations on 
the overall site plan for the Municipal Complex. Each option integrates a 10’ civic spine 
walkway along the former Thomes right-of-way (R.O.W.) defined with pedestrian pavers, as 
well as a number of other elements consistent with the site planning program including the 
following: 
•	 relocated memorial
•	 central gathering area
•	 employee gathering patio
•	 enhanced entry to the Civic Center
•	 beer garden on the north side of the Civic Center
•	 enhanced storm-water facilities
•	 foundation planting to reduce maintenance
•	 potential northwest corner stair tower access to the Cox Parking Facility
•	 enhanced east/west connection
•	 enhanced lighting
Framework Concept 1
This framework option emphasizes a large central lawn gathering space for events between 
the Municipal Building and the Cox Parking Facility. The civic spine walkway is aligned to 
utilize some of the existing walkway system. A memorial plaza is shown at the intersection 
of the Civic Spine and the connecting walkway between the Municipal Building and Cox 
Parking Facility
Framework Concept 2 - Preferred
Framework Concept 2 creates a large central plaza space for events, gathering and 
memorial integration. The civic spine facilitates a more natural arcing movement from and 
to the Municipal Building and is emphasized with pedestrian pavers.
Framework Concept 3
This option attempts to utilize as much existing pedestrian infrastructure as possible 
while meeting project goals in an effort to reduce costs. However, in doing so, movement 
patterns are less efficient and natural than option 1 and option 2. A memorial plaza is 
located to the west of the proposed stair tower to the Cox Parking Facility.

-16-
Outreach and survey process
A survey was provided during the initial public workshop and on-line following the 
workshop with graphic materials in order to assess preferences for the site plan framework 
options, parking configuration options and overall site character. The following pages show 
survey results and preferred options for each project component. There were 112 survey 
participants, some of whom were City Staff working in the Municipal Complex.
Parking options survey
Parking configuration option 3 was most preferred by survey partcipants. This option 
maximizes additional parking and provides an aligned access point and bus parking 
adjacent to The Burke Senior Center. Removal of the existing storage building is required 
to achieve the proposed parking quantity
Framework concepts survey
Framework Concept 2 was preferred by survey participants, emphasizing a larger event 
plaza at the nexus of the civic spine and connecting walkway between the Municipal 
Building and the Cox Parking Facility.  
Character survey
The character survey shows a preference toward simple, more natural materials such as 
stone, understated site furnishings, tivoli lighting to enhance safety and character and a 
memorial concept that involves sandblasting in seatwall caps.

Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan
Character Survey

-21-
Final Plan 
The final plan strives to create a character and identity which is distinctive and unique 
through its design, materials and features and relates to the Civic Commons Park design.  
The plan uses materials such as native plants, stone, brick and simple site furnishings to 
create a timeless and permanent quality that will not seem dated after a few years, but will 
remain a valued asset to the community.  
Strong pedestrian connectivity with alignments that flow with natural movement patterns 
are emphasized while allowing most existing trees to remain. The storm-water drainage 
facility is converted to a water quality swale similar to a dry stream in character in order to 
slow down velocity while improving water quality. 
The perception of safety and a feeling of overall comfort is created through enhanced 
visibility and lighting. This is accomplished through minimizing middle-story planting 
focusing on overhead planes, and elements below eye-level. In order to achieve this, 
pruning of lower tree branches is recommended. In addition, effective lighting design 
will be critical to this effort, and represents an opportunity to utilize newer, more efficient 
technologies that shed light to surfaces rather than the sky. Removal of the existing storage 
building near the north parking lot enhances visibility of the throughout the complex, 
both from 22nd Street and from the interior of the complex while improving the overall 
impression of the space, 
Memorial and Plaza
The three existing memorials should be unified and simplified through sandblasting on 
stone seat wall caps. The constitution could be sandblasted or relocated to a central 
location within the plaza. The plaza is spatially emphasized through overhead tivoli lighting 
and a planting island allows a degree of private and public seating options. The space 
allows for 3-4 event tents to be provided if desired.
Civic Center Entry
In response to the Civic Center plans, the entry should be enhanced with some 
reconfiguration. The current drop-off location is shown relocated further south along West 
20th St. to allow for gathering to occur at the proposed entry location. An enhanced 
crossing is shown at the intersection of West 20th St. and Thomes Ave. Tivoli lighting 
emphasizes the entry and the entry to the civic spine.
The proposed beer garden between the Cox Parking Facility and Municipal Building allows 
for limited access from the Municipal Building for use by employees as a gathering space 
when not in use. During Civic Center events, the beer garden provides a new amenity for 
visitors.
