Section B

-28-
The following pages outline additional information on the final plan to provide a site 
planning direction for the overall project.
Intersection Bulb-out Treatments
In order to accomplish the goal of creating an interconnected civic spine utilizing Thomes 
Ave. to connect to other municipal facilities, a prototype plan with intersection treatments 
is shown in order to decrease pedestrian crossing distances and create a strong sense of 
place along Thomes Ave.  
Lighting Plan
The conceptual lighting plan depicts an arrangement of pedestrian pole lights along 
primary and secondary walkways. Lighting arrangement is organized to achieve light levels 
on walkway surfaces that achieve approximately a minimum of 1 foot-candle or greater in 
key areas, such as the central plaza and along the civic spine. Luminaires should be full cut-
off LED fixtures matching those in the Civic Commons to achieve an overall unity within the 
Civic District. 
Planting Strategy
Foundation planting is shown along building edges in order to reduce lawn maintenance 
and water use while providing a visual anchoring effect for the existing structures and 
creating a visual transition to the ground plane. Planting should emphasize native or low 
water use species that are hardy, low maintenance and provide year round interest.
Phasing and Implementation
Implementation of the final plan is envisioned in two phases. Phase I should be 
coordinated with the Civic Center improvements and include the memorial plaza. Phase 
II is envisioned as the north parking enhancements, continuation of the civic spine to the 
Laramie County Library and the NE/SW pedestrian connections.

Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan

-35-
Appendix
Future Coordination with Cheyenne Housing Authority
The Municipal Complex Pedestrian Plan dated April 2020 prepared by Russell-Mills for the 
Cheyenne MPO is a concept plan for the predominant public space located between the 
City Municipal Building, Civic Center, Cox Parking Garage, and the North City Parking lot. 
However, some space represented in the Plan is on private property owned by the Burke 
High Rise and the Cheyenne Housing Authority (CHA). The maps below generally represent 
the areas described above. It is understood that the improvements shown are a general 
Concept Plan and are not funded as a construction or final design project.  Additionally, 
the timing or schedule for the project is not known.  If the project is funded, in part or its 
entirety; the City, as the manager of the design and construction  shall include the CHA 
in the final design and construction processes.  Any improvements that are  implemented 
from the Plan within the CHA private property shall be paid for by the funding source being 
utilized for the project. One intention and benefit of the Plan is to increase the number of 
parking spaces in the North Parking Lot.  We understand the CHA would like to secure a 
specific number of parking spaces in this North Parking Lot for Burke High Rise residences 
and CHA employees and patrons. It is understood that the number of spaces will be 
negotiated during the project’s final design process. 
Municipal Complex Pedestrian Routing Plan

PLANNING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION:  At their regular meeting on July 6, 2020, the 
Planning Commission recommended approval of an exception to the block size requirement of UDC 
4.3.3.a, and approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing Body, 
by a vote of 3-0, with the condition that a revised final plat map addressing the following conditions be 
submitted prior to Council approval: 
• 
Revise the final plat to show widen of the internal access easement to a width to accommodate a 
street section to the satisfaction of the City Traffic Engineer. 
• 
Align the internal access easement to connect to the intersection of Mulberry and Nationway. 
• 
Prior to City signing of final plats, utility drawings demonstrating compliance with Board of Public 
Utility construction and looping standard shall be reviewed and approved by the Board of Public 
Utilities. 
(Staff met with the applicant after the Planning Commission mailing and before the Planning Commission 
meeting. At this meeting it was determined by Staff that it was not essential to address the condition 
regarding drainage at this time [Condition #3 below]. The Planning Commission subsequently did not 
include that condition in their recommendation.)

STAFF RECOMMENDATION (6/29/2020):  Staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat and 
the exception to block size requirement of UDC 4.3.3.a, with the following conditions: 
1. Revise the final plat to show widen of the internal access easement to a width to accommodate a 
street section to the satisfaction of the City Traffic Engineer. 
2. Align the internal access easement to connect to the intersection of Mulberry and Nationway. 
3. Prior to Governing Body consideration of the expedited final plat, a drainage study shall be 
submitted to the City and said study shall be reviewed and approved by the City Engineer.   
4. Prior to City signing of final plats, utility drawings demonstrating compliance with Board of Public 
Utility construction and looping standard shall be reviewed and approved by the Board of Public 
Utilities.

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00162 
4 
 
 
 
 
 
EXPEDITED REVIEW: 
This plat is being pursued as an expedited application.  An expedited review process is a concurrent 
preliminary and final plat. A project may be eligible for an expedited review when the proposal contains 
no significant planning, design or engineering issues.  In other words, proposals that meet all applicable 
regulations and generally meet the review criteria for preliminary and final plat may be eligible for the 
concurrent preliminary and final plat process.   
  
Due to meeting the expedited plat criteria set forth in Section 2.1.2.c(3) of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code, this plat has proceeded as an expedited application. 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
 
This plat proposes platting nearly 10 acres of previously unplatted land into 2 lots. The subject property 
has concurrently applied for a zone change to MUB Mixed-use Business Emphasis. The specific 
development proposed with the plat is a multifamily (senior housing) facility on about 2.8 acres. The 
remaining acreage is reserved for unknown future development, but could become residential, 
commercial, or a mix of uses under the proposed MUB zone. 
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this plat. The comprehensive plan encourages infill development which 
maximizes the use of existing infrastructure. The plat also contributes a potential mix of new commercial 
and residential uses to an activity center. 
 
Surrounding properties are largely already developed with commercial and residential uses. There is 
limited opportunity for this plat to tie into the properties to the west and east, but this plat can provide a 
north-south connection linking Lincolnway and Nationway for vehicles and pedestrians. The proposed plat 
would not have an undue influence on surrounding development.   
 
The applicant has provided an access easement to connect Lincolnway and Nationway, but the location 
and width of the access easement still may need modification to provide an effective connection. The 
drainage area and discharge proposed by this development is along Henderson Ditch, and further 
information is required to determine if the proposed drainage mitigation measures would be effective. 
Utilities need to be extended to service this development, and the applicant has indicated the intent to do 
so, but final alignments and extensions need clarification. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria set forth in Section 2.1.2.d and 2.1.3.d, staff recommends 
the approval of the Preliminary Plat. 
 
 
 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00162 
5

PRELIMINARY PLAT DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and 
decisions on a proposed preliminary plat, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following 8 criteria, as found in Section 2.1.2.d:
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with 
the development policies and physical patterns identified 
in PlanCheyenne.  
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this proposed plat.  
 
Multiple policies and principles within PlanCheyenne 
encourage infill development which maximizes the 
use of existing infrastructure. While the subject 
property will have to extend utilities to effectively 
service development, extensions are relatively minor, 
and all utilities exist in the area. The subject 
property is surrounded by previous development.  
 
The development of the subject property for multi-
family senior housing furthers the objective of 
Principle 2.6 in PlanCheyenne, which states, “Our 
community will contain housing for senior citizens and 
other populations with special needs, truly making it a 
place for all generations.”  
 
The property is currently subject to a recently-adopted future land use map amendment re-
designating the area as “Mixed-Use Commercial Emphasis,” which will support the proposed zone 
change for the property, the proposed residential development on Lot 1, and a mix of uses with 
future development on the proposed vacant lot. 
 
 
2. PLANNING - The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to 
existing and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities.  
 
This plat proposes creating 2 lots on previously unplatted land. The first lot is proposed for immediate 
development as a senior housing multifamily development. The second lot has no immediate plans 
for future development and is intended to be replatted at a later date when development plans are 
more specific. The location of utilities, access, and drainage features on the second lot used to 
service the first may create constraints to future development; however any development of Lot 2 
would be aware of the location of these constraints and should be able to work within them. Because 
Lot 2 would be required to be replatted, modification of some of these facilities may be possible 
when Lot 2 develops. Properties surrounding this development are largely already developed or 
platted and this plat would not create any undue influence on potential development or 
redevelopment of these surrounding areas. 
 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00162 
8 
 
3. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - Division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in 
Article 4. 
 
Transportation Network: 
The block on which this property is located is larger than allowed by current block allowances. 
Surrounding development patterns provide little opportunity for connections. However, this 
property is adjacent to both Lincolnway and Nationway. The division of the property would allow 
a local connection between these two streets. The block standards of the Unified Development 
Code suggest a public street would be optimal, but large commercial development sites may 
propose access easements instead of full public streets as an alternate option. The applicant has 
proposed a 30’ wide access easement linking north-south, but the location and width of the 
access easement is deficient. 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
The proposed plat contains previously unplatted land. Plat fees are required at $350 per acre for 
the Land Acquisition Fee, $350 per acre for the Infrastructure Fee, and the Residential 
Enhancement Fee is $400 per unit (due at time of Building Permit). Lands platted for 
governmental use, public utilities, or as undevelopable are exempt, as is any land dedicated for 
public rights-of-way. For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest 
tenth of an acre. The required fees for the 2.8 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as 
follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
$980 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
$980 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees:  $1,960.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Projected Residential Enhancement Fee 
$22,400 (56 units at $400 per unit) 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: 
$1,960.00 
 
Open Space: 
During the plat process, this property is not required to provide open space. The applicant has 
indicated site-specific open space would be provided with site development. 
 
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development. 
Easements are provided for water and sewer connections to service the lots within the proposed 
plat area. 
 
 
 
 
 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00162 
9 
 
4. PHASING - Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public facilities of the 
subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the likelihood of future phases being 
constructed as planned. 
 
Lot 1 is proposed to be developed and Lot 2 is proposed as an outlot. An outlot is undevelopable until 
subsequent platting action. Lot 1 requires access to utility services and the existing utilities are on the 
far ends of the plat boundary. Development of Lot 1 would ‘front load’ these utilities, making the the 
future of the development of Lot 2 more likely. However, placing utilities for Lot 1 may also provide 
some constraints to the development of Lot 2. 
 
 
5. STUDIES - The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or 
requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the application, can 
mitigate these impacts. 
 
The applicant submitted studies addressing drainage and traffic. Redivisions to these studies were 
also provided after the first round of Staff review comments. The drainage needs further evalutation 
with respect to how this development would impact Henderson ditch. No further studies were 
requested with this application and no other concerns were identified by Staff. 
 
 
6. ZONING - All proposed lots shall be buildable lots 
according to the existing or proposed zoning district 
standards, and result in site design and development 
patterns that meet the Intent and Applicability 
statements of the existing or proposed zoning district. All 
parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not 
dedicated to and accepted by the City shall have a clearly 
defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance. 
 
The area contained within this plat is currently zoned 
CB Community Business, but is going through the 
process to be changed to MUB Mixed-use Business 
Emphasis. The buildable lot proposed by this 
development is appropriately sized for development 
in either zone district. 
 
The applicant is proposing leaving the remaining area as 
an outlot, as no specific future development is proposed. 
Outlots are unbuildable lots, but are slated for future 
development.  
 
Map #4 – Current Zoning  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00162 
10 
The MUB zone is intended for a mix of uses in a compact development pattern and is intended to be 
a central component of activity centers. This property is located near the intersection of Ridge, 
Lincolnway, and Nationway and in an area with a diverse mix of commercial and residential 
development. Due to the nature of exisiting surrounding development, there is limited opportunity for 
this property to provide the compact block network intended by the MUB zone. The plat does have 
the opportunity to better meet the intent of the zone district by providing a public street or 
accessway connecting Lincolnway and Nationway. 
 
 
7. SURROUNDING DEVELOPMENT - The application will not create any undue influence on existing or 
potential development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan 
policies for that property. 
 
Property surrounding this proposed plat is largely already developed. To the west, east and 
northeast, properties are already developed with commercial uses. To the south and north, nearby 
development is largely residential in nature, but these residential areas are separated from the 
proposed plat area by large streets and the drainage ditch. The large lots proposed by this plat are 
similar to some of the commercial lots in the area. The proposed plat would not have an undue 
influence on surrounding properties. 
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
The proposed plat requires alignment of the accessway, a drainage study, and appropriate utility 
provision. As these issues are resolved, Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not otherwise 
adversely affect the public health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
 
REVIEW 
 
The following agencies were asked to review the proposed project: 911/Combined Communications, 
Black Hills Energy, Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), Building Department, CenturyLink, Charter, 
Community Recreation and Events, Emergency Management, Engineering, Environmental Health 
Department, Fire Department, Laramie County Assessor, Laramie County Attorney, Laramie County Clerk, 
Laramie County School District #1, Metropolitan Planning Office, and Planning and Development.

developed rate. An outfall depth/discharge rating curve is required showing that the outlet 
structure is in full compliance for storm events ranging from the 2- through 100-year events. 
Because the outfall is directed to the Henderson ditch, an evaluation of lag time for the ditch and 
on-site drainage should be conducted to show that flow from the developed site doesn’t increase 
peak flow in the ditch. Evaluation of downstream conveyance may be required as well.

EXPEDITED REVIEW: 
This plat is being pursued as an expedited application.  An expedited review process is a concurrent 
preliminary and final plat. A project may be eligible for an expedited review when the proposal contains 
no significant planning, design or engineering issues.  In other words, proposals that meet all applicable 
regulations and generally meet the review criteria for preliminary and final plat may be eligible for the 
concurrent preliminary and final plat process.   
  
Due to meeting the expedited plat criteria set forth in Section 2.1.2.c(3) of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code, Staff recommends allowing the plat to proceed as an expedited application. 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
The proposed platting action is intended to re-arrange lot lines around existing structures to allow the 
individual sale of structures. The original development was multiple 2-unit buildings built as a multi-family 
project over lot lines on a large ownership parcel. The two-unit buildings could function as duplexes if 
subdivided and appropriately provided with separate utility services.  
 
The original residential project was not built to current standards and as there is no intention to demolish 
the existing development. As such, variances were required to allow the existing structures within the 
proposed subdivision. These variances were approved for the existing structures, but any redevelopment 
of these lots would be expected to meet current standards.   
 
As the existing structures are expected to remain, the influence on the surrounding neighborhood by this 
platting action is expected to be minimal. The street network, utilities, open spaces, drainage patterns, 
are all pre-existing. Both the future land use designation and current zoning reflect the existing conditions 
at this site. 
 
The applicant also owns land about a block away which is in a similar situation and is concurrently going 
through the platting process as Stanfield Addition, 5th Filing.  
 
Due to meeting the review criteria set forth in Section 2.1.2.d and 2.1.3.d of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code, staff recommends the Planning Commission approves this 
expedited plat, advancing the final plat to the Governing Body.

PRELIMINARY PLAT DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and 
decisions on a proposed preliminary plat, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following 8 criteria, as found in Section 2.1.2.d:
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in PlanCheyenne.  
 
PlanCheyenne is supportive of this proposed plat.  
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, 
the subject land has been designated as Urban 
Residential. The Urban Residential future land use 
designation encourages a variety of residential uses 
and densities as the primary development pattern. 
The existing development patterns within and around 
the plat boundary meet these expectations and the 
proposed plat would not adversely affect the 
neighborhood. 
 
Principle 7.2 of PlanCheyenne encourages growth to 
be coordinated in such a way that services and 
utilities are adequate to service development and 
provided in a timely manner. While the property is 
already developed, the utility service lines require  
re-arrangement to allow the individual sale of the land. 
 
 
2. PLANNING - The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to 
existing and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities.  
 
This plat proposes creating 3 new lots from 4 pre-existing lots (which were combined into one 
ownership parcel). The original development built four 2-unit buildings as a multi-family development 
on these 4 lots without respect to the internal lot lines. The proposed platting action would  
re-arrange the lot lines in such a way that 2 of the existing structures would be on their own lots, and 
the remaining 2 structures would be on one shared lot. There is no proposal to demolish the existing 
buildings or redevelop the site. The street network, utilities, open spaces, drainage patterns, are all 
pre-existing and the proposed plat would not have an undue influence on the neighborhood. 
 
 
3. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - Division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in 
Article 4. 
 
Transportation Network: 
The plat proposes dividing land within a previously-approved street network – no additions or 
subtractions from the transportation network are suggested. 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Expedited Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00174 
7 
Community Facility Fees: 
This land was platted as part of Stanfield Addition in 1923. While plat fees did not exist in 1923, 
this plat is exempt from paying plat fees as it meets the requirements of UDC 4.2.5.c.6:  
“Where land is being replatted, it shall be exempt if: 
(a) The replat consists only of reconfiguring or eliminating interior lot lines with no 
additional land or building area added to the development; 
(b) The replat results in no additional dwelling units, residential units, non-residential 
units or lots, and therefore causes no greater impact on public park facilities than the 
original plat; and 
(c) The replat results in no change in zoning, density, or intensity of use than the original 
plat.” 
 
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: 
$0.00 
 
Open Space: 
During the plat process, this property is not required to provide open space. This plat qualifies for 
an exception (UDC 4.4.2.b). 
 
Required Improvements: 
This division can provide all required utilities to the properties within this development.  
Re-arrangement of water and sewer service lines will be required if the lots are to be sold off 
individually. 
 
 
4. PHASING - Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public facilities of the 
subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the likelihood of future phases being 
constructed as planned. 
 
No phasing is proposed with this plat. 
 
 
5. STUDIES - The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or 
requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the application, can 
mitigate these impacts. 
 
The applicant submitted a traffic worksheet and drainage worksheet. No other studies were 
requested with this application and no concerns were identified by Staff with the information 
provided. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

Expedited Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00174 
8 
 
6. ZONING - All proposed lots shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed zoning district 
standards, and result in site design and development patterns that meet the Intent and Applicability 
statements of the existing or proposed zoning district. All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and 
which are not dedicated to and accepted by the City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for 
ongoing maintenance. 
 
The proposed lots are buildable lots in the proposed 
zone district. Variances were sought and granted to 
allow the existing buildings to remain on the proposed 
lots, but if the existing structures were demolished, 
new, compliant structures could be built on these lots. 
 
The MR Medium-density Residential zone is intended 
for a mix of residential uses with full access to city 
services. The existing development pattern meets the 
intent and applicability of the MR zone and the 
proposed plat does not propose modifications to the 
current development. 
 
No lots are proposed as unbuildable. 
 
 
7. SURROUNDING DEVELOPMENT - The application will not create any undue influence on existing or 
potential development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan 
policies for that property. 
 
Surrounding development is largely residential in nature. Nearby properties contain a mix of single-
family, duplex, and multi-family style development. To the east, the land is open space near Crow 
Creek. The current development patterns and those proposed by this plat are consistent with the 
Comprehensive Plan’s expectations for this area of the community. The proposed plat does not create 
an undue influence on the surrounding area.  
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, or general 
welfare.

9 
 
FINAL PLAT DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and decisions on 
a proposed final plat, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the following 3 criteria, as 
found in Section 2.1.3.d:  
1. The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved preliminary 
plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, and any 
deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the Comprehensive 
Plan. 
2. The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat.  
3. The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. 
As this plat is being pursued as an expedited application, review criteria 1 and 2 are not applicable. 
Review criterion 3 is also found in the Preliminary Plat process (as criterion 3) and is covered in 
subsection 3 above. 
 
 
REVIEW

Community Recreation and Events, Emergency Management, Engineering, Environmental Health 
Department, Fire Department, Laramie County Assessor, Laramie County Attorney, Laramie County Clerk, 
Laramie County School District #1, Metropolitan Planning Office, and Planning and Development.

ii
MEMO
TO:
Mayor Marian J. Orr and members of the City Council
FROM:
Charles W. Bloom, AICP
SUBJECT:
Declaration of Surplus Property
DATE:
July 13, 2020
The attached Resolution declares a portion of Holliday Park as surplus property to allow it to go
to public sale in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-112(A). If the resolution passes it will
authorize the City to advertise the surplus property and for interested parties to respond.
The subject property is located on the northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and Lincolnway within
Holliday Park. The property is presently developed with a parking lot and is physically
separated by a stone wall on the north and east. The parking lot is typically not utilized for park
users and needs substantial upgrades including subgrade reconstruction, repaving, and ADA
improvements.
The area proposed for surplus designation is approximately 0.52-acres in size and has not been
surveyed. Based on Laramie County Assessor records, the land value in the area is $9.40 per
square foot. Staff estimates the value of this land to be approximately $212,912. Staff will
require that the winning bidder survey the property to clearly delineate the land to be sold. The
final purchase price will be based on an appraisal and final square footage of they surveyed lot.
The Governing Body will have the ability to review all bids and can elect to accept one or reject
all. Upon acceptance of the bid, staff will commence drafting of a Purchase Agreement that will
require review and approval by the Governing Body.
The Purchase Agreement will detail the
purchaser’s responsibility to subdivide the land from the park, provide an appraisal justifying the
price, appraised value of the land, and solidify the development proposal.

AprovetJ as to
forn ony
RESOLUTION NO.
Date
L1?rO
ENTITLED:
“A RESOLUTION DECLARING AS SURPLUS A CITY-OWNED
PROPERTY GENERALLY LOCATED AT THE NORTHEAST
CORNER OF MORRIE AVENUE AND EAST LINCOLNWAY,
TOGETHER
WITH
IMPROVEMENTS
THEREON,
AND
AUTHORIZING THE CITY TO PROCEED WITH THE PROCESS
TO DISPOSE OF THE PROPERTY PURSUANT TO WYO. STAT.
§ 15-1-112(A).”
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne (“City”) is the owner of a 24,000 sq. ft., more or less,
property (“Property”) generally located at the northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and East
Lincoinway as generally shown in Exhibit A which is attached hereto and incorporated herein;
and
WHEREAS, the Property is a portion of property within Holliday Park and is partly
developed with a parking lot, is isolated from the developed park by a stone retaining wall, is in
need of repair and upgrades, and is not necessary for park functionality; and
WHEREAS, in accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-1 12(A), the City is required to provide
public notice of a proposed sale of the Property, shall request sealed bids proposing a purchase
price and proposed use of the property, and has the right to reject all bids if desired; and
WHEREAS, within sixty (60) days of bid award, the winning bidder and the City shall
execute a Purchase Agreement which shall require approval by the Governing Body; and
WHEREAS, the Purchase Agreement shall detail subdivision responsibilities to subdivide
the land from the park, appraised value of the land, and proposed use of the property and all costs
for subdivision and appraisals shall be borne by the winning bidder; and
WHEREAS, it is acknowledged that the exact legal description of the property has not yet
been prepared, and if declared surplus, will be surveyed prior to purchase pursuant to the terms of
the Purchase Agreement, will not exceed 28,000 sq. ft. in size and will generally be the area shown
in Exhibit A; and
WHEREAS, the City, having determined that the Property is not necessary for retention
or expansion of City-related interests, desires to declare the aforementioned Property as surplus,
and desires to initiate the process for sale of Property.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the aforementioned Property generally located at the
northeast corner of Morrie Avenue and East Lincoinway, Cheyenne, Wyoming, as generally
shown in Exhibit A, together with improvements thereon, is hereby declared as surplus, and the
City is authorized to proceed with the bidding process to dispose of the Property pursuant to Wyo.
Stat. § 15-1-112(a).

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that upon completion of the bidding process, the City shall present
for consideration by the Governing Body any and all qualified bids received for formal action. The
City reserves the right to reject any or all bids.
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS
DAY OF
2020.
MARIAN J. ORR, MAYOR
(SEAL)

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
4 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:   
Whitney Ranch is expected to be a large-scale development encompassing over 550 acres between Dell 
Range Boulevard and Storey Boulevard, west of Whitney Road. However, only portions of the property 
are ready for development in the near future, and this plat proposes platting a relatively small portion in 
the southwest corner of the larger property. The proposed plat contains about 50 acres of currently 
vacant county land. The land is proposed to be annexed to the City and be zoned MR Medium-density 
Residential. The proposal includes 107 single-unit residential lots and 7 lots for open space, trails, and 
detention. This area of the community contains a significant ridgeline and topographic change. The 
topography creates constraints with proposed development and platting.  
 
PlanCheyenne is largely supportive of this plat. The subject property and surrounding area are designated 
Urban Transition Residential and Urban Residential. The Urban Transition Residential encourages a mix of 
lower-intensity housing types ranging from large rural residential uses to smaller lot single-family homes 
and duplexes. Policies and principles found in the comprehensive plan support interconnected residential 
development with open spaces and full connection to City services. This plat proposes connecting into 
established neighborhoods, a connected internal network, and preservation of significant topographic 
features. PlanCheyenne encourages the use of existing infrastructure and the timely provision of new 
infrastructure – this plat is part of a large development on the edge of the community and infrastructure 
improvements are needed for adequate service.  
 
The proposed plat is adjacent to existing developments to the south and west. The plat proposes tying 
into these existing neighborhoods. The plat proposes only limited connections to the north, but 
topographic constraints limit the opportunity for effective connections. Lots proposed by this plat are 
similar in size to the existing development patterns and the plat ties into the street stubs left by previous 
neighborhoods’ development. The plat would not have an undue influence on the surrounding properties. 
 
Multiple blocks within this proposed subdivision do not meet the block standards put forth in the code; 
however, there are allowances made for constrained sites. Due to topographic conditions and open space 
provided, the Natural Features or Open Space exception may be applicable. Such exceptions must be 
approved by Council. The plat also proposes lots which have frontages on parallel streets. Lots of this 
nature can create issues with access, addressing, setbacks, and maintenance. To mitigate some of these 
issues, the applicant has proposed that the HOA be the agency responsible for maintenance along Van 
Buren and adding a note to the plat prohibiting lots from being accessed off of Van Buren. This lot 
arrangement must also be approved by City Council, which may require further conditions to mitigate the 
adverse impacts of such a lot configuration. 
 
The applicant has submitted materials addressing drainage, traffic, and utilities, and it appears this plat is 
able to provide all required improvements. Further detail will be required with the final plat and with 
construction. The applicant proposes providing trail corridors and large natural areas to meet the open 
space requirement. 
 
Due to meeting the review criteria set forth in Section 2.1.2.d of the Cheyenne Unified 
Development Code, staff recommends approval of the Preliminary Plat.

PRELIMINARY PLAT DATA:  In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and 
decisions on a proposed preliminary plat, the Staff, Planning Commission and City Council shall use the 
following 8 criteria, as found in Section 2.1.2.d:
 
1. PLANCHEYENNE - The application is in accordance with 
the development policies and physical patterns identified 
in PlanCheyenne.  
 
PlanCheyenne is generally supportive of this 
proposed plat.  
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, 
the subject land has been designated as Urban 
Transition Residential. Surrounding areas to the north 
and east are also so designated. The areas to the 
south and west are designated Urban Residential. 
The Urban Transition Residential future land use 
designation encourages lower-intensity residential 
uses, but still encourages a mix of housing types. The 
mix of housing types expected ranges from large 
rural rural residential uses to smaller lot single-family 
homes and duplexes. Higher-intensity uses (such as 
apartments) are not expected. The adjacent Urban 
Residential designation encourages a range of more intensive residential uses. While the proposed 
zoning of MR Medium-density Residential would allow higher intensity residential uses, the proposed 
plat does not propose such uses. 
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response to 
market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns proposed by 
this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 2 of PlanCheyenne encourages new residential development to be compatible and 
connected with existing residential neighborhoods. The proposed plat ties into the adjacent 
neighborhoods and, where adjacent to established neighborhoods, proposes lots of similar size.   
 
Foundation 4 of PlanCheyenne encourages a connected, multi-model transportation system that can 
accommodate future growth. The proposed plat connects to existing developments, but is 
constrained by topography to the north. A proposed trail system increases the potential for 
pedestrian and cyclist connectivity. 
 
Foundation 6 of PlanCheyenne encourages the creation of open space and trails and the preservation 
of significant natural resources. While the plat will be modifying and building along the ridge line in 
some areas, two large open space parcels propose preserving other portions and a trail system is 
proposed through the development. 
 
Map #3 – Future Land Use  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
8 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne support the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision of 
services (including emergency and education services). Large developments on the edge of the 
community can create issues with providing services in an effective and timely fashion. Development 
of this area of the community creates the need to improve street and emergency service 
infrastructure (see conditions #3 and #4). 
 
 
2. PLANNING - The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to 
existing and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities.  
 
The proposed plat is adjacent to existing developments to the south and west. The plat proposes 
tying into these existing neighborhoods. The plat proposes only limited connections to the north, but 
topographic constraints limit the opportunity for effective connections. The plat proposes an open 
space system in the topographically challenging areas, which would allow for an integrated open 
space and trail system with future developments. The preliminary plat shows the applicants intent to 
tie into and extend utilities appropriately. The proposed plat would not create an undue influence 
upon existing or future development in the area. 
 
 
3. SUBDIVISION STANDARDS - Division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in 
Article 4. 
 
Transportation Network: 
Whitney Ranch is expected to be a large-scale development encompassing over 550 acres 
between Dell Range Boulevard and Storey Boulevard, west of Whitney Road. However, only 
portions of the property are ready for development in the near future, and this plat proposes 
platting a relatively small portion in the southwest corner of the larger property. The applicant 
has submitted a Transportation Network Plan for the entire site showing how the conceptual 
street network would layout for the entire site. Network plans are required to show how a large 
development would conceptually comply with current street master plans. The network plan 
should also show how the proposed local street grid could conceptually layout. Finally, the 
network plan shows how a new development ties into existing developments and deals with 
constraints. The Transportation Network Plan provided by the applicant fulfills these objectives. 
The proposed plat largely fits into the proposed network plan. The network plan shows a cul-de-
sac coming north off of the current terminus of James Drive, but this plat truncates that street 
with an open space parcel (see condition #1d). The Transportation Network Plan is attached to 
this Staff Report. 
 
Maximum and minimum block dimensions are established by the Unified Development Code. 
Multiple blocks within this proposed subdivision do not meet the block standards put forth in the 
code; however, there are allowances made for constrained sites. On level land, connections 
northward from Blocks 3 and 7 would be expected; however, due to the significant topographic 
change, Staff recommends that both of these blocks qualify for the Natural Features exception. 
Block 4 is also larger than allowed by the code, but has a 200’ wide open detention area in the 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
9 
middle of the block with a proposed trail. With this trail connection and open area, Staff 
recommends that this block qualifies for the Open Space System exception. 
 
Single tier blocks are generally prohibited, but may be allowed with specific City Council approval. 
Single tier blocks contain lots known as ‘double frontage lots’ or ‘though lots’ and are lots with 
frontages on two parallel streets – one street in the front and one in the rear of the lot. Lots of 
this nature can create issues with access, addressing, setbacks, and maintenance. In this plat, 
there are lots along either side of Van Buren which fit this description. To mitigate maintenance 
issues, the applicant has proposed that the HOA be the agency responsible for maintenance 
along Van Buren. To mitigate some of the other issues presented by single-tier blocks, the 
applicant has added a note to the plat prohibiting lots from being accessed off of Van Buren. The 
City Council may also “require additional design elements either in the right-of-way or easements 
to mitigate the adverse impacts of double frontage lots and blocks” (UDC 4.3.3.b.2). 
 
According to the code, similar lot types should face each other across the public street. Large lots 
are facing significantly smaller lots across Carla. However, the transition proposed is from smaller 
single-unit parcels to larger single-unit parcels (a relatively small step in intensity/density). 
 
The rights-of-way proposed with this plat meet standard street sections and match the widths of 
the surrounding development. 
 
Van Buren is designated as a Collector and is one of few connections anticipated to go through 
the ridgeline. It is also anticipated to connect from Dell Range to Storey in the future. Limiting 
access onto Collectors is important for safety purposes. The proposed plat includes a note stating 
that the double-frontage lots in Blocks 3 and 6 would not access Van Buren; but, for clarity, the 
corner lots on Block 2 and 5 should also be so limited (see condition #2). 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
The proposed plat contains previously unplatted land. Plat fees are required at $350 per acre for 
the Land Acquisition Fee, $350 per acre for the Infrastructure Fee, and the Residential 
Enhancement Fee is $400 per unit (due at time of Building Permit). Lands platted for 
governmental use, public utilities, or as undevelopable are exempt, as is any land dedicated for 
public rights-of-way. For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest 
tenth of an acre. The required fees for the 17.6 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as 
follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
$6,160 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
$6,160 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees:  $12,320.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Projected Residential Enhancement Fee 
$42,800 (107 units at $400 per unit) 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Total Fees due at signing of the plat: 
$12,320.00 
 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
10 
Open Space: 
Open space is required to be provided with this subdivision. For residential development, open 
space is required at 750 square feet per dwelling unit or 8% of the gross plat area (whichever is 
less). This plat proposes 107 single-unit parcels and has a gross acreage of 48.6 acres. The 
required open space is 80,250sqft. The applicant proposes providing trail corridors and large 
natural areas to meet the open space requirement. At over 10 acres in size, the proposed Lot 9, 
Block 1 (reserved as a natural area) meets the minimum area requirement for a natural area and 
also fulfills the required open space area. 
 
Required Improvements: 
This proposed subdivision appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the properties 
within this development. The preliminary plat indicates appropriate utility stubs outs to allow 
service to future development. New traffic control improvements may be necessary as Whitney 
Ranch develops, and this portion of the overall development should contribute to such 
improvements.  
 
 
4. PHASING - Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public facilities of the 
subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the likelihood of future phases being 
constructed as planned. 
 
Phasing proposed with this plat is limited to open space development. No phasing is proposed for the 
development of the residential lots. Open space dedication is required as part of a platting action, 
and the applicant proposes a trail system to service this project. The open spaces within the 
residential portion of this plat are proposed to be developed with the residential areas. However, a 
portion of the open space and trail system is along the northern and eastern boundaries of this plat 
and are proposed for future phased development, as these areas may be affected by future platting 
actions. A phased approach to the development of the open spaces located north and east of the 
core residential areas of this plat is logical, but may decrease the probability of being developed as 
planned.  
 
 
5. STUDIES - The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or 
requested to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the application, can 
mitigate these impacts. 
 
The applicant submitted an addendum to a previously-conducted traffic study for this project, a 
Conceptual Drainage Analysis, and an Environmental Impact Assessment.  
 
The drainage analysis provided a conceptual model for drainage, but a more detailed study is 
required with the final plat. The study should document the flow routing between the proposed 
detention ponds and available capacity downstream (see condition #1a). 
 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
11 
The traffic study and letter will need to be updated to correctly reflect the proposed Dell Range and 
Whitney Road intersection upgrade options (see condition #1b). 
 
No other studies were requested with the Preliminary Plat application. 
 
 
6. ZONING - All proposed lots shall be buildable lots 
according to the existing or proposed zoning district 
standards, and result in site design and development 
patterns that meet the Intent and Applicability 
statements of the existing or proposed zoning district. 
All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are 
not dedicated to and accepted by the City shall have a 
clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing 
maintenance. 
 
The property is currently zoned A-2 in the County. 
The project is proposed to be annexed to the City 
and zoned MR Medium-density Residential. The MR 
zone is intended to develop with a mix of 
residential types and densities. A highly-connected 
street network within ½ mile of activity centers is 
encouraged for this zone district. The plat proposes 
a connected street network, except where the 
topography on the north constrains the 
development. The plat is less than ½ mile from Dell Range, but the nearest existing activity center is 
more than ½ mile away at College and Dell Range. The plat proposes a mix of residential density, 
with duplex and single-family unit types.   
 
The applicant has proposed 107 single-unit residential lots and trail and open space lots. The 
proposed residential lots are all buildable lots in the proposed MR Medium-density Residential zone 
district. If developed as single-family homes, lot types range from DD7 to DD5. All lots could also be 
developed as SD1 (duplex) lots or AD1 (townhome lots) in this zone district. 
 
Lot 9, Block 1; Lot 9, Block 3; Lots 5 and 6, Block 4; Lot 8, Block 6; and Lots 1 and 20, Block 7 are 
proposed as unbuildable lots. The applicant has designated these lots for open space, trails, or 
detention and has indicated they will be owned and maintained by the HOA.  
 
 
7. SURROUNDING DEVELOPMENT - The application will not create any undue influence on existing or 
potential development for adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan 
policies for that property. 
 
Surrounding properties to the south and west are largely already developed with medium density 
residential uses in the MR zone. Lots proposed by this plat are similar in size to the existing 
development patterns and the plat ties into the street stubs left by previous neighborhoods’ 
Map #4 – Current Zoning  
Source: Cheyenne/Laramie County GIS 
Cooperative Program 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
12 
development. As with any new residential development, neighboring property owners who are used 
to living near undeveloped land will have to acclimate to the loss of views and the use of formerly 
dead-end streets. However, this plat proposes tying into multiple neighborhoods and connecting to 
multiple streets, diffusing the impacts of the plat on the existing developments.  
 
The areas to the north and west of this plat are currently vacant and have significant topographic 
features. The proposed plat does not provide very many connections in these directions, but due to 
the topographic constraints, opportunities for effective connections are limited. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates area around the plat boundary as Urban Residential and Urban 
Transition Residential. The medium-density single-family residential pattern proposed by this plat is 
appropriate for this mix of designations. 
 
The plat would not have an undue influence on the surrounding properties.  
 
 
8. PUBLIC HEALTH, SAFETY, AND WELFARE – Whether the application will harm the public health, safety or 
general welfare. 
 
The proposed grades on the land north of this development exceed 30% in some places. Not all 
materials are stable at such grades; however the applicant has indicated that a geotechnical study 
has been performed and that the proposed grades would be stable with the soils and geology found 
at this site. The applicant also submitted an Environmental Impact Assessment which did not identify 
any undue influences. Staff is satisfied the proposed plat would not otherwise adversely affect the 
public health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
 
REVIEW 
 
The following agencies were asked to review the proposed project: 911/Combined Communications, 
Black Hills Energy, Board of Public Utilities (BOPU), Building Department, CenturyLink, Charter, 
Community Recreation and Events, Emergency Management, Engineering, Environmental Health 
Department, Fire Department, Laramie County Assessor, Laramie County Attorney, Laramie County Clerk, 
Laramie County School District #1, Metropolitan Planning Office, and Planning and Development. 
 
COMMENTS FROM REVIEWING AGENCIES ON THE FIRST SUBMITTAL: 
- 
(FYI) Plat fees applicable to this plat. 
- 
(Justification provided in Response to Comments) Site is topographically challenging and 
this proposal includes areas exceeding 30% slope. Soils analysis should be provided to show the 
material would be stable under such conditions. 
- 
(FYI) Project connects into street stubs left by adjacent developed areas appropriately. 
- 
(Condition) Conditions of Preliminary Plat approval will be included to detail expectations, 
timing, and funding of the following:  
o 
Property acquisition, on site or off site, for a fire station to serve the development. 
o 
Property acquisition, on site or off site, for an emergency siren to serve the development.   
o 
Installation of a traffic signal at Dell Range Boulevard and Van Buren Avenue. 
o 
Necessary drainage studies and/or documents required as part of final plat/engineering 
plan review submittal.   

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
13 
- 
(Fulfilled by revised submittal) The BOPU does not support or approve the 20’ BOPU 
Easement between lots 11 and 13, Block 7. 
-

pursuant to International Fire Code, Appendix “C”. 
-

submitted with this plat isn’t sufficient. An updated traffic study is required. 
-

maximum block dimensions. However, some blocks may meet requirements for exceptions or 
alternative compliance due to topography. Please justify. 
- 
(Justification provided in Response to Comments) UDC 4.3.3.b (1&2): single-tier lots are 
proposed on either side of VanBuren. Single-tier lots create issues with setbacks, sidewalk 
construction and maintenance, lot access, fencing, etc. These should be avoided, but exceptions 
may apply. Optimally, the western side of VanBuren would have rear-loaded lots and Garnet Way 
would be moved further west to allow lots to allow two tiers of lots along that stretch; while 
Lasso would be moved further east to also allow two tiers of lots. Please revise or justify. 
- 
(Fulfilled by revised submittal) Lots along Van Buren may not be able to be accessed off of 
Van Buren - a note on the plat may be needed. 
- 
(Fulfilled by revised submittal) Greenway Comments: Per future plans for the Greater 
Cheyenne Greenway it appears that GW is intended to head south from future Storey and to 
follow the west property line, ultimately crossing the property along the ridge to Whitney. As you 
work your way thru platting of the entirety of the proposed Whitney Ranch development please 
try to address best options for an easement to provide for the public greenway. The greenway 
should be separated from roadways as much as possible and meet </=5% grade. It is unclear 
from maps provided that this has been considered. If there are any current ideas and or concepts 
you have been working on please share with Jeanie Vetter, Greenway Planner (307-638-4379). 
- 
(Fulfilled by revised submittal) Trail corridor lots are disconnected and likely not to be 
developed with this plat. Recommend removal from the plat boundary or the inclusion of a more 
cohesive corridor. 
- 
(Justification provided in Response to Comments) It appears that Lot 9, Block 3 is also 
intended to be used as a drainage conveyance. Please widen this lot to accommodate both trail 
and conveyance and include appropriate drainage easement. 
- 
(Fulfilled by revised submittal) The Open Space Narrative indicates the Final Plat would 
include area outside of the Preliminary Plat boundary to satisfy open space requirements. Please 
include all land to be final platted within the Preliminary plat. 
- 
(FYI) The future land use designation for the proposed preliminary plat is Urban Transition 
Residential. Urban Transition Residential calls for a limited range of lower density residential 
uses, blending urban and rural standards. It allows single family residences and multi-family 
duplexes, patio homes, and townhomes. Urban Transition Residential supports the proposed uses 
of this plat, but calls for lower density than that being proposed. With that being said, as the 
subject property comes into the City and is rezoned, it may be beneficial to amend the future 
land use map to redesignate the area (all of Whitney Ranch) as Urban Residential. Though the 
current Future Land Use Designation generally supports the residential use of this proposal, the 
proposed density is better suited for an Urban Residential category. Additionally, the MR Zone 
district is listed as a “most applicable zone district” under the Urban Residential Future Land Use 
category.

COMMENTS FROM REVIEWING AGENCIES ON THE TRAFFIC STUDY AND UPDATE LETTER: 
- 
(Condition) Throughout the study the intersection of Dell Range Blvd & Whitney Rd is shown as 
a signalized intersection. The signalized intersection is the alternative. Please show information 
with Dell Range & Whitney as a roundabout. Please include both – showing first the roundabout 
and second a signalized intersection option. 
- 
(Condition) The US 30 study recommended a roundabout with turn lanes. This study states a 
single lane roundabout.  
 
COMMENTS FROM REVIEWING AGENCIES ON THE SECOND SUBMITTAL: 
- 
(FYI) Justification for Blocks 3, 4, and 7 appears logical. Blocks 3 and 7 have topological 
challenges. Block 4 is effectively split by a large open space parcel. Council approval required 
(UDC 4.3.3.c). 
o 
[Original Comment] UDC 4.3.3.a: Multiple blocks exceed maximum block dimensions. 
However, some blocks may meet requirements for exceptions or alternative compliance 
due to topography. Please justify. 
o 
[Applicant Response] The developer request exceptions for block perimeter around 
blocks 3 and 7 due to the fact that they are backing up to hillsides and open space. Block 
4 is requesting an exception due to the fact that a pedestrian access and open space are 
splitting the parcel that will be owned and maintained by the HOA. 
 

Preliminary Plat | UDC-20-00177 
15 
- 
(FYI) Justification for single-tier lots appears logical - HOA maintenance would assist in one of 
the significant issues presented by single-tier blocks. Council approval required (UDC 4.3.3.b.2) 
o 
[Original Comment] UDC 4.3.3.b (1 &2): single-tier lots are proposed on either side of 
Van Buren. Single-tier lots create issues with setbacks, sidewalk construction and 
maintenance, lot access, fencing, etc. These should be avoided, but exceptions may 
apply. Optimally, the western side of Van Buren would have rear-loaded lots and Garnet 
Way would be moved further west to allow lots to allow two tiers of lots along that 
stretch; while Lasso would be moved further east to also allow two tiers of lots. Please 
revise or justify. 
o 
[Applicant Response] Rear loaded lots are not desirable for today's buyers. Due to the 
fact that the Development will have an HOA, developer proposes to have the HOA 
maintain the sidewalks and landscaping along Van Buren Avenue where rear yards abut 
roadway. With Van Buren's designation of a collector this would also decrease parking 
and congestion along Van Buren. Alley's would also need an exit route and be additional 
maintenance for City crews in the future. 
- 
(Condition) Corner lots adjacent to Van Buren in Block 2 and Block 5 should also have no 
access to Van Buren. 
o 
[Original Comment] Lots along VanBuren may not be able to be accessed off of 
VanBuren - a note on the plat may be needed. 
o 
[Applicant Response] The developer agrees that no lots will be accessed off of Van 
Buren and will add a note to the preliminary and final plat. 
- 
(FYI) Justification for not providing similar lots across a public street is not convincing. However, 
the transition proposed is from smaller single-unit parcels to larger single-unit parcels (a 
relatively small step in intensity/density).  
o 
[Original Comment] UDC 4.3.3.b.5 - similar lot types should face each other across 
the public street. Large lots are facing significantly smaller lots across Carla. Please 
justify. 
o 
[Applicant Response] The developer is trying to balance the number of lots in each 
category in each section of Whitney Ranch to keep diversity in each section. As this is a 
new development in most situations of change not meeting this requirement are 
happening internal to the project. The lots need to stay consistent within blocks as the 
land and future grading will convey water from north to south. The development needs 
consistent north south property lines for easier drainage easements and conveyance. 
- 
(Condition) Newly provided Open Space lot: Lot 20, Block 7 cuts off a portion of James Drive 
and is inconsistent with the Network Plan. Recommend modification, removal, or an alternate 
designation (eg. outlot). 
- 
(Condition) Be advised that a detailed drainage study/plan will be required with submission of 
the Final Plat and should adhere to the requirements listed in Ordinance No. 4087. The study 
should include a detailed hydrologic model and detailed hydraulic models documenting the flow 
routing between the proposed detention ponds in series and the available capacity in the 
downstream existing storm sewer in Connie Drive. The study will need to document/verify the 
required detention storage for the site as well as on-site conveyance of flow.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2020-07-01_TotalExtraction_88653982.pdf
City of Cheyenne
ORIGINAL
GRANT AGREEMENT BETWEEN
WYOMING DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH, AGING DIVISION
AND
CITY OF CHEYENNE, OFFICE OF YOUTH ALTERNATIVES
Parties. The parties to this Grant Agreement (Agreement) are Wyoming Department of
Health, Aging Division (Agency), whose address is: 2300 Capitol Avenue, Hathaway
Building 4th Floor, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82002, and City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth
Alternatives (Grantee), whose address is: 1328 Talbot Ct. Cheyenne, WY 82001.
2.
Purpose of Agreement.
The purpose of this Agreement is to set forth the terms and
conditions by which the Grantee shall provide services for the Foster Grandparent Program
(FGP), a state funded programs supporting older adults in Wyoming. The FGP services
provide stipends and benefits to as many senior volunteers over the age of fifty-five (55)
as possible so they may continue to remain self-sufficient and independent in their
community for as long as possible. The service areas for this program are Goshen, Laramie,
and Platte.
3.
Term of Agreement.
This Agreement is effective when all parties have executed it
(Effective I)ate). The term of the Agreement is from July 1. 2020 or the Effective Date,
whichever is later, through August 30, 2021. All services shall be completed during this
term. The period of performance for Grant activities is from July 1. 2020 or the Effective
Date, whichever is later, through June 30, 2021. Grantee must utilize all funds associated
with this Agreement during the period of performance.
4.
Payment.
A.
The Agency agrees to pay the Grantee for the services described in Section 5 below
and in Attachments A and B, both of which are attached to and incorporated into
this Agreement by this reference.
Total payment under this Agreement shall not
exceed twenty-eight thousand, two hundred seventy-two dollars ($28,272.00).
Each month the Grantee shall submit an invoice for services rendered in the prior
month. Payment shall be made within forty-five (45) days after submission of
invoice pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 16-6-602.
Grantee shall submit invoices in
sufficient detail to ensure that payments may be made in conformance with this
Agreement.
B.
No payment shall be made for work performed before the Effective Date of this
Agreement.
Should the Grantee fail to perform in a manner consistent with the
terms and conditions set forth in this Agreement. payment under this Agreement
may be withheld until
such time
as the Grantee performs
its duties and
responsibilities to the satisfaction of Agency.
C.
Except as otherwise provided in this Agreement, the Grantee shall pay all costs and
expenses, including travel, incurred by Grantee or on its behalf in connection with
Grant Agreement between
Wyoming Department of Health, Aging Division
and City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives
Page
1 of9

Grantee’s performance and compliance with all of Grantee’s obligations under this
Agreement.
5.
Responsibilities of Grantee. The Grantee agrees to:
A.
Provide the services described in Attachments A and B as approved by the Agency

Attachment A
Statement of Work
This document is intended as a Statement of Work (SOW) to identify and describe important milestones and
deliverables for the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives (Grantee) for the Foster Grandparent Program
(FGP) grant agreement for State Fiscal Year 2021.
Foster Grandparent Program
The core mission of the Foster Grandparent Program is to provide stipends and benefits to as many senior
volunteers over the age of fifty-five (55) as possible so they may continue to remain self-sufficient and
independent in their community for as long as possible. The scope of the program is fostering self-sufficiency and
independence in seniors over the age of fifty-five (55) by providing opportunities to engage in community
volunteerism that directly benefits school-aged children, while receiving a stipend and other benefits.
There are five (5) goals in the grant application that the Grantee must strive to achieve within the one (1) year grant
period (period of performance defined in Section 3 of the Agreement). All of these goals have methods of tracking
the progress on a monthly basis.
To be eligible for the program a client must be fifty-five (55) years of age or
older and residing in Wyoming, and, through an ongoing evaluation, be financially eligible to participate in the
program, (at least two hundred percent (200%) of poverty level or lower). The services provided for the service
area clients have specific tracking mechanisms and projected service goals to achieve during the one year grant
period.
This program serves Goshen, Laramie, and Platte counties.
Foster Grandparent Program
The service deliverables include maintaining or increasing by up to two percent (2%) the number of unduplicated
volunteers, volunteer hours, volunteer satisfaction and reducing the wait-list of volunteers, tracking the number of
U. S. veterans who are recruited and enrolled as volunteers, maintaining or increasing by one percent (1%) the
number of children who benefit from the program. The Foster Grandparent Program will strive to maintain the cost
efficiency of providing stipends and benefits per volunteer per year by tracking program costs and funds. The
provider will report all potential or suspected adult abuse, neglect or exploitation to the appropriate authorities.
Each month the Grantee shall submit an invoice, for services rendered in the prior month to the Community Living
Section (CLS) Program Manager by the fifteenth (15th) day of the month. The Grantee shall submit the quarterly
program and financial reports to the CLS Program Manager as outlined in the Timetable of Payments and
Reporting Requirements chart in this Attachment A.
After the grant application has been approved by the Foster Grandparent Program Manager, any revision to any
category (Personnel, Travel, Equipment, Consumable Supplies or Other) that exceeds 10% of the entire grant
amount (i.e. Entire Grant Amount
— $30,000 x 10%
= $3,000), must be submitted in writing to the Foster
Grandparent Program Manager. Revisions requests must be made by May 15, 2021. Once approved, the Grantee
will submit the categories budget sheets to the Foster Grandparent Program Manager. The approved changes will
become part of the approved grant application.

AMENDMENT TO CITY CONTRACT NO. 7155
SPONSORSHIP AGREEMENT BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
AND SPRADLEY BARR TOYOTA
This agreement amends City Contract No. 7155, dated March 25, 2020, between the City of
Cheyenne, Wyoming, and Spradley Barr Toyota, to replace the current language in paragraph 5,
Events, with the following language:
5.
Events.
A.
As a 2020-2021 Platinum Venue Sponsor to assist in purchasing a film projector, in
exchange for Thirty-Five Thousand Dollars ($35,000.00) of sponsorship revenue to the City of
Cheyenne, Spradley Barr Toyota will receive:
•
Promotional vendor space at all 2020-2021 film series shows (i.e., one car outside
of Civic Center) two hours prior to the show and removed following the show.
•
Four Facebook posts, two organic and two sponsored.
•
Logo recognition on one indoor banner.
•
Logo recognition on one exterior venue banner.
•
Promotional material may be distributed to film series attendees (i.e., flyer, keychain,
etc.). Promotional material must be provided by the sponsor to the Cheyenne Civic
Center marketing team one week prior to showing.
•
Logo recognition in out-of-town marketing endeavors. (E-mail blasts to out of
town/state patrons. E-blasts are distributed to approx. 30,000 people.)
•
Four comp tickets per film series show.
•
Pre-recorded announcement before each show. Sponsor will be recognized by name
in the pre-show overhead announcement. 
•
Sponsor commercial to be played before each film and in the lobby. Assets must be
provided by the sponsor to the Cheyenne Civic Center marketing team one week
prior to showing.
All other terms and conditions of the existing agreement shall remain unchanged and in full
force and effect.

MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Marian J. Orr, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Doug Klahn, Construction Manager  
 
DATE: 
June 10, 2020 
 
SUBJECT: 
Contract Modification #3 to Simon Contractors, for the 2018 Mountain Road and 
20th Street Pavement Rehabilitation Project.   
 
 
Please find the attached proposed contract modification for the above referenced city project.   
Construction and Engineering Staff have discussed the unforeseen condition of the underlying road 
surface of 20th Street from Evans Avenue to Bent Avenue and determined that it will require 
additional preparatory work to achieve a quality project. The additional cost associated with these 
repairs is estimated at $119,428.80. The project is carrying an approximate balance of unused 
funds in the amount of $70,000 therefore, the requested amount for this modification is $65,000.   
In addition, this will require an extension of the completion date to July 3, 2020. Staff respectfully 
requests your approval of this contract modification.

AGREEMENT

THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this
____
day of
,
_____,
by and between
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and SIMON
CONTRACTORS, hereinafter referred to as the “CONTRACTOR”.
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein,
mutually agree as follows:
ARTICLE 1.
STATEMENT OF WORK.
The Contractor shall furnish all supervision,
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike
manner in the construction of the “Prairie Avenue & Frontier Mall Drive Intersection” project
all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents including all addenda thereto, numbered and
dated: Addendum One, June 1, 2020, Addendum Two, June 8, 2020.
ARTICLE 2. RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY. The Contractor shall in any
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract or any other
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative.
ARTICLE 3. TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION. The services to be performed under
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued
by the City. The work shall be completed by the following: Substantial Completion: November
15th, 2020, Minor Punchlist Items: July 1st, 2021. If the work has not been completed within the
time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable delays,
the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is
completed.
ARTICLE 4. COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT. The CITY will pay
the Contractor for the performance ofthe Contract in current funds, the sum ofOne Million, Three
Hundred Thirty-Nine Thousand, Eight Hundred Forty-Six Dollars and Fifty Cents
($1,339,846.50).
In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid
Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract
amount will be adjusted accordingly. The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation:
a.
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for percentage of estimated work completed.
Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the payable due date as established
annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request shall be submitted on the Contract
Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or the AlA Documents G702 and G703.
The engineer will review the estimate for approval prior to payment.

b.
The City will withhold ten percent (10%) of the dollar value of the work completed for a
minimum of ten (10) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement has been published in
accordance with Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-113(h). Upon completion of the work under this Contract,
the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; the Consent of Surety;
Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, Suppliers and
Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a current Workers
Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above documents have
been received by the City and all items on the Punch List have been completed, and the
advertising requirements have been met.
ARTICLE 5. CONTRACT. The executed Contract Documents shall consist ofthe following:
1.
This
Agreement,

the
attached Exhibit A;

Provisions; and

This Agreement, together with other documents enumerated in this Article 5, which said
other documents are as fully a part of the Contract as if hereto attached or herein repeated, forms
the Contract between the parties hereto.

ATTACHMENTS
